# Hayba UE MCP — Supertooling Roadmap

> **Generated 2026-06-28** from a 45-agent analysis workflow (2.86M tokens, ~29 min). **31 domains · 1054 proposed tools** (P0:290 P1:400 P2:294 P3:70).
>
> Competitive sources: Epic UE 5.8 first-party MCP, Aura/Ramen, Flopperam/Flop, GenOrca (253), chongdashu, ChiR24, remiphilippe, runreal, and cross-DCC peers (Blender/Houdini/Maya/Unity). Internal audit: current ~70 first-class tools, two P0 editor-freeze bugs, the add-a-tool refactor gate.
>
> Companion files: `2026-06-28-mcp-supertooling-tools.json` (machine-readable catalog) and `2026-06-28-mcp-competitive-and-audit-appendix.md` (raw research).

# Hayba UE MCP — Executive Roadmap: The Most Powerful Production-Grade Unreal MCP

## 1. Executive Summary

The Unreal MCP landscape changed twice in June 2026. Epic shipped a **first-party MCP in UE 5.8** — the free, sanctioned front door every agent will reach for by default. And **Flopperam consolidated into Aura** ($40M, 30k devs, an in-editor agent with self-correcting C++ live-compile). Hayba is now a third-party server competing against a first-party baseline *and* a funded incumbent. Raw breadth alone does not win this: Aura ships ~50–64 tools, GenOrca 253, UAIP ~730. We will not out-count UAIP, and we should stop pretending tool count is the scoreboard.

We win on a different axis: **production-grade safety + agent ergonomics + worldbuilding depth that Epic's baseline explicitly will not build** — then we close the breadth gap to "hundreds, honest." Three internal-audit truths gate everything below and must be said plainly, because the alternative is shipping the same dishonesty we are criticizing competitors for:

1. **The "126 tools" headline is partly false advertising.** ~70 tools are genuinely first-class. `blueprint_add_node` returns `not_implemented_in_v1`. ~100 working C++ commands have *no* first-class wrapper and are reachable only through `hayba_invoke ue_legacy` or `python_run`. The honest practical surface today is "python_run plus guessing."
2. **We are not production-ready for unattended use.** Two P0 editor-freeze bugs — `build_*` (300s) and `test_run` (120s/test) block the game thread — plus SEH coverage that wraps only the python and material handlers, leaving 31 of 33 handlers unrecoverable on a crash.
3. **Adding one tool touches 4–7 files across two languages plus a plugin rebuild**, with the same schema re-declared in four places that drift. The blitz cannot begin until the add-a-tool path is refactored.

This roadmap groups the **1054 designed tools across 31 domains** into five shipping phases. Phases 0 and 1 are hard gates: no net-new tool ships until the freezes are fixed and the scaffolding is collapsed to one descriptor. We target **~240–290 honest first-class tools** — surpassing GenOrca's 253 — with crash-resilience, transactional one-undo, PLUMB quantified validation, token-economized routing, and a revived BYOK in-editor copilot that none of them match.

The opinionated thesis: **the scaffolding refactor is the whole game.** Every count in the domain digest is achievable only if a tool becomes *data declared once* and most of them ship as `pyTemplate` descriptors with zero plugin rebuilds. Get Section 4's refactor right and the 1054 tools are a sequencing problem. Get it wrong and we churn 7 files per tool and never finish.

**The differentiator we own and no single competitor has all of:** local + crash-resilient + transactional + token-economized + quantified-constraint-validated + worldbuilding-native + BYOK. That sentence is the product. Everything in this roadmap reinforces at least one clause of it.

---

## 3. Vision & Defensible Moats

The vision is the **production-grade Unreal worldbuilding agent**: an agent can build, validate, and ship a playable, populated UE5 world unattended — and when it crashes, the editor survives; when it mutates, it validates before and after; when it errs, one Ctrl+Z undoes the whole step; and the human can watch and steer from an in-editor chat panel they pay for with their own key.

Five moats. Every design agent must reinforce at least one; none may dilute them.

### Moat 1 — Crash-resilience & lifecycle hardening
The `HaybaGameThread::RunSync*` seam, ticker-based TCP dispatch (avoids task-graph re-entrancy), SEH guard, dangling-delegate gating (#283/#284), and the 1MB frame cap are reliability machinery **no competitor documents**. The single biggest lever is extending the SEH guard from python/material to the `ProcessCommand` seam so all 33 handlers become recoverable. This is what makes unattended loops safe — and it is invisible until you compare crash logs.

### Moat 2 — PLUMB quantified validation + constraint language
The 23-tool PLUMB stack (profile bake/annotate, constraint define/propose/validate, masks, lessons, study, grammar-expand, productions, sockets, SAM segment) is **closed-loop "validate before/after mutate" with a real constraint grammar**. It is strictly beyond Flop's auto-repair rules and Houdini's graph-intelligence. This is our worldbuilding correctness engine: green/red plan-mode overlay, unsat-core surfacing, lessons-as-memory. Every write domain — `mesh-and-geometryscript` topology verdicts, `foliage-and-scatter` density constraints, `landscape-and-terrain` slope/height checks, `water-system` water-below-terrain audits — routes through it.

### Moat 3 — Token economy / Code-Mode / deferred routing
Code Mode exposes 5 meta-tools and discovers the rest on demand (~3K tokens vs hundreds of schemas — a ~95% cut). Deferred routing uses a BM25+embedding tool index, packs, and `hayba_invoke` polymorphic dispatch. **This is the prerequisite for the breadth strategy** — it is the only way to ship hundreds of tools without drowning the context window. Epic shipped a lighter version (Tool-Search), so we speak their meta-verbs (`list_toolsets`/`describe_toolset`/`call_tool`) for interop while keeping the deeper index.

### Moat 4 — `python_run` universal escape hatch (hardened)
SEH + 3-tier security gate + dangling-delegate gate. This is the de-facto API surface. But the postmortem is blunt: ~90% of real sessions hand-rolled python because typed primitives never covered the author loop. The strategic move is **not to celebrate the hatch** — it is to convert python patterns into typed `pyTemplate` tools (Section 4). The hatch becomes the dispatch substrate, not the user-facing answer.

### Moat 5 — Vision-capture feedback loop
`editor_capture_viewport`, `render_camera`, PIE screenshot, the SceneCapture world-pos pass, and the visual sidecar (CLIP score, moodboard, SAM segment) are strongest-in-class. We return PNG as image content blocks. The gap: multi-angle/orbit, thumbnails, buffer-viz passes, auto-frame, before/after diff (Section 5, theme 4B). Critically, the critic flagged **~16 near-duplicate capture verbs** across domains — these must share one parameterized capture core, not 16 drifting implementations.

## 4. The Phased Roadmap

The 1054 designed tools collapse into five phases. **The ordering is non-negotiable**: Phase 0 (stop freezing/lying) and Phase 1 (the scaffolding refactor) are gates. Phases 2–4 are the breadth blitz, sequenced by ROI: cheapest-highest-value first.

A blunt warning before the phases: the critic identified **~40+ near-duplicate wrappers** that will drift and confuse tool selection — per-domain `*_undo`/`*_transaction`, `*_validate`, `*_capture`, `*_inspect`, and console/cvar verbs re-declared in ~10 domains each. **These must be built as cross-cutting seams, not per-domain copies.** Transaction/undo, capture, validate, dry-run, console/cvar, reflection-search, and material-assignment each get exactly one implementation that domains call. This is a Phase-1 design decision; if we defer it, the 1054 count inflates with garbage and we ship the same incoherence we mock in GenOrca.

### Phase 0 — STOP LYING / STOP FREEZING (P0 gate, blocks everything)

Goal: become honestly production-safe for unattended use. No net-new tool ships before this lands.

- **Fix the two freeze blockers.** Convert `build_*` (300s) and `test_run` (120s/test) to the job-envelope pattern in `docs/audit/2026-06-22-mcp-async-command-conversions.md` — return `{job_id, status:"running"}` immediately, poll for completion. Because `python_run` handlers also run on the game thread, **every long subprocess (UBT compile, cook, UAT BuildCookRun, GatherText, MovieRenderQueue) must stay C++ behind a shared `FHaybaAsyncJobRegistry` with a `*_job_status` reader.** This single registry serves `project-build-cook-packaging`, `sequencer-and-cinematics` render, `localization` commandlets, and `lighting` movie-render — build it once.
- **SEH-wrap `ProcessCommand`.** One seam → all 33 handlers recoverable. The biggest reliability lever for the smallest diff.
- **Harden transport.** Re-discover port on reconnect; backoff reconnect; align executor timeouts to UE ceilings (2/10/60s vs UE 300/120/600s); **gate the blind retry to idempotent commands only** (else duplicate `actor_spawn`/`actor_delete`); wire the dead `auth` field and `timeout` code.
- **Stop the false advertising.** Fix `blueprint_add_node` (Phase 4) *or* remove it from the catalog now. Prune `packs.yaml` of unregistered tool refs. Unify the error envelope (render/build currently escape the one-failure-shape invariant). Gitignore `hayba-memory.db`. Establish the **honesty invariant**: every advertised tool works or is flagged not-callable. No second `not_implemented_in_v1`.

### Phase 1 — ADD-A-TOOL REFACTOR (P0 gate — the multiplier)

Goal: make a tool *data, declared once*, so the remaining ~140–190 net-new tools land without per-tool 7-file churn. This is the gate that makes "hundreds, honest" feasible. Today there are 104 `server.tool` calls vs 32 descriptors (~30% migrated), with schema re-declared in wrapper Zod, descriptor Zod, sidecar params, and C++ `TryGet*Field`.

1. **One descriptor as sole authoring surface.** Extend `ToolDescriptor`: `{name, domain, cost, meta, schema, returns, alwaysOn, destructive}` plus exactly one of `ueCommand` (synthesizes the passthrough handler — kills wrapper files), `pyTemplate`, or `handler`. Derive pack from `domain` (delete `inferDir`); derive always-on from the flag (delete both `register.ts` lists).
2. **Auto-collect descriptors.** Each domain folder exports `descriptors[]`; a generated barrel concatenates them. Kills the 470-line literal and 140-line import block.
3. **Generate the sidecar** from descriptors at build (`deriveSignature` already does Zod→param docs); replace the existence-only lint with "generated == committed."
4. **`pyTemplate` factory = the vehicle for hundreds.** A parameterized python snippet with validated params bound in, dispatched through the hardened `python_run` handler → schema-validated, discoverable, journaled tools with **zero plugin rebuilds**. This is the workhorse for `actor-level-editing`, `mesh-and-geometryscript`, `materials-and-textures`, `foliage-and-scatter`, `audio-and-metasounds`, `water-system`, `world-partition`, `datatables-curves-tags`, and most of the rest.
5. **C++ declarative command table** (only when hot-path C++ proliferates): `TMap<FString,FCommandSpec>` with param types, a `destructive` flag, and a lambda; add `mcp_describe_commands` so the sidecar generates from the running plugin (closes field-level drift) and kills the `IsDestructiveCommand` hardcoded-list bug class.

Sequence: (1)+(2)+(3) are pure-TS, no rebuild → ship first. Then (4). Then (5) last. The `mesh-and-geometryscript` and `editor-introspection` domains are the factory's first heavy customers and should be built alongside it as proof.

### Phase 2 — SURFACE THE TIER-2 DEBT + QUICK PY WINS (cheap, highest-ROI breadth)

Goal: roughly double the honest catalog with thin wrappers and no-rebuild python, while leading with the inspection-first reads that make every later write safe.

- **Surface ~100 stranded C++ commands** as first-class tools (TS wrapper + descriptor + sidecar entry, no rebuild): `level_*`, `seq_*`, `anim_*`, `foliage_*`, `spline_*`, `physics_*`, `input_*`, `ui_*`, `gas_*`, `bt_*`, `data_*`, `project_*`, `wp_*`. The `sequencer-and-cinematics`, `animation-and-controlrig`, `UMG`, `enhanced-input`, `AI-navigation`, and `GAS` domains all have working-but-buried C++ handlers — surfacing them honestly is the single cheapest breadth gain. **Fix the silent-stub honesty violations here**: `foliage_add_instance`/`foliage_paint_at` (return success, persist nothing) and `seq_export` (returns an error).
- **Quick python wins** (no rebuild, high value): `nanite` (easiest in the list), `lighting-postprocess` Lumen presets (easy, high-value), multi-select/batch + selection management (`actor-level-editing`), `foliage-and-scatter` asset authoring, `asset-import` Interchange/USD outcome-verbs, `audio-and-metasounds`, `datatables-curves-tags`, and `water-system` outcome-verbs (`water_river_from_path`, `water_ocean_preset_apply`).
- **`editor-introspection-and-observability` reflection layer** lands here too — class/property/function/enum/struct discovery closes the postmortem's "no reflection-introspection tool" gap that forces 90% of sessions into guessing. This unblocks every other domain's agent.

### Phase 3 — SAFETY & ERGONOMICS MUST-HAVES (the moats, productized)

Goal: turn the moats into named, shippable features that beat Epic head-to-head. (Detailed in Sections 5–6.)

- **Transactions/undo (cross-cutting, C++).** Wrap every mutating handler in `FScopedTransaction`; expose begin/end/undo/redo/checkpoint/restore. The named differentiator: **multi-step bundling into ONE Ctrl+Z** (`run_tool_script`-style), vs Epic's per-call undo. Generalize the `dry_run→commit` pattern that today only `world_generate` has. **This is the single cross-cutting seam** that retires the ~10 per-domain transaction duplicates.
- **Vision-loop expansion** (theme 4B): orbit/turntable, thumbnails, auto-frame, buffer-viz passes (depth/wireframe/normals/basecolor/overdraw), before/after diff — all on **one parameterized capture core** retiring the 16 duplicate capture verbs.
- **Batch ops** (theme 4D): selection management, batch transform/property-set, rename, material-assign, align/distribute, find-replace-class. Few high-level outcome-verbs, matching the Roblox peer's `mass_set_property`/`search_by_property`.
- **Revive the BYOK in-editor copilot** (Section 5).

### Phase 4 — HARD C++ HIGH-VALUE + DISTRIBUTION

Goal: build the genuinely-hard capabilities and ship to market.

- **Blueprint K2 graph authoring** (`blueprint-authoring`) — the largest false-advertised capability. Honestly implement `blueprint_add_node`/`connect_nodes`/`add_event` on the EdGraph/EdGraphSchema_K2 plumbing the handler already imports; ship `blueprint_build_graph` as ONE transaction + dry-run + PLUMB validate.
- **PhysicsAsset/Chaos** (`physics-and-chaos`) — fracture, geometry collections, fields, constraints; net-new moat no competitor has. Job-envelope the simulate-and-bake.
- **Landscape sculpt/weightmap-paint** (`landscape-and-terrain`) — new `HaybaMCPLandscapeHandler` over `FLandscapeEditDataInterface`; lead with the **Gaea→UE bridge** using the repo's existing Gaea corpus no rival has.
- **ControlRig and Niagara** — accept the ceilings honestly: scope to template-instantiation + exposed-param packs, flag as engine limitation (do not fabricate unstable graph-authoring calls — that is how `blueprint_add_node` happened).
- **Distribution** (theme 6): prebuilt binaries, widen `EngineVersion` beyond pinned 5.7.0, remove engine-private-header includes (or honestly document non-marketplace status), Fab metadata, Epic-compatible `.mcp.json`.

### Net-new domains slotted across Phases 2–4

The critic flagged ~13 entirely-missing domains. Slot by competitive value: **Phase 2** — `datatables-curves-tags`, `water-system`, `gameplay-framework-defaults` (gating: nothing is playable until GameMode/World Settings are wired), classic `level/map management`, `enhanced-input` authoring. **Phase 3** — `source-control` (biggest unblocker for team-safe unattended edits — an agent mutating 40 assets must check them out and submit a coherent changelist), `UMG` (`umg_compose_tree` matching Roblox `create_ui_tree`), `editor-introspection` trace/Insights capture. **Phase 4** — `GAS`, `AI-navigation` (BT/Blackboard/NavMesh/EQS/perception), `networking-replication` (single-process multi-client PIE assertion harness — the load-bearing constraint is `RunUnderOneProcess=true`), `virtual-textures`, `localization`. Niche backlog (not P0): Take Recorder, Media Framework, Groom, Chaos Cloth/Vehicles, Alembic/GeometryCache, Decals, gameplay Volumes.

## 5. Reviving the In-Editor BYOK AI Copilot Chat Panel

This is a named mandate and a true differentiator: a local, BYOK, multi-provider agent loop running *inside the editor*, plan-mode-gated, with vision context — against Aura's cloud-tier lock-in and Epic's no-panel baseline.

**Architecture decision (locked): thin C++ client, fat Node server.** Put the agentic tool-calling loop in the Node MCP server. C++ becomes a streaming SSE chat *client*. Do **not** rebuild tool-calling in C++ — that duplicates the catalog, gating, validator, and journal, and risks game-thread deadlock. The `ai-copilot-chat-panel` domain (34 tools) splits into three layers: sidecar tools (provider/key config, the streaming loop, plan-mode gating, session persistence), introspection/verify/dry-run tools (so the agent can self-check the panel without guessing), and C++ components (Slate panel, key vault, SSE cancel).

**Concrete plan:**

1. **Restore `llm-client.ts` from commit `ac46d40`** — the only correct tool-calling shape in repo history (`LLMTool`/`LLMToolCall`/`LLMResponse`, `stopReason 'tool_use'|'end_turn'`). Add `@anthropic-ai/sdk` and `openai` deps plus streaming.
2. **Restore the 8-provider catalog** into a single `providers.ts`: merge the `2849a75` presets (`mock, anthropic, groq, openrouter, openai, ollama, lmstudio, custom`) with the `4d47e58` env/baseURL/model maps.
3. **Add `POST /chat/stream` (SSE)** to the existing Express app in `dashboard/api.ts` (new `chat-server.ts`). It runs the loop over the *live* registry, honoring `DisabledTools`, archetype `tool_filter`, and the **Plan-Mode gate** (`bPlanApproved`) before any destructive tool. Add per-session token-budget accounting and feed Code-Mode deferred routing into the loop (token-economy moat).
4. **C++ side:** provider dropdown + **DPAPI-encrypted key storage** (kill the plaintext `GEditorPerProjectIni`); switch `FHaybaMCPClaudeClient` to consume SSE; implement the promised `Cancel()`.
5. **Reuse what's already production-grade:** the Slate `SHaybaMCPChatPanel`, the `OnToolCallRecorded` observability seam, and the tool-stream/validator/diff panels. Wire Preview→Plan panel; add session persistence; auto-attach viewport/selection as vision context.

The moats this presses, in one panel: token economy (budget + deferred routing), crash-resilience (loop runs server-side, never on the game thread), secure-local BYOK (DPAPI vault vs competitors' plaintext/cloud), and vision feedback (auto-attached context).

---

## 6. Production-Readiness Gate

"Production-ready" is a checklist, not an adjective. Nothing ships to market until all of it is green.

- **Crash-resilience:** `ProcessCommand` SEH-wrapped (all 33 handlers recoverable); dangling-delegate gate live; 1MB frame cap; ticker-based dispatch; `RunSync` seam for every handler that touches the game thread. **No handler may `WaitFor`-block the game thread** — long ops go through `FHaybaAsyncJobRegistry`.
- **Honesty invariant enforced in CI:** a lint that fails the build if any advertised tool lacks a working handler or a `not-callable` flag. This is the rule that prevents a second `blueprint_add_node`.
- **Tests:** the live-editor harness (Section 7) runs the full catalog against a real editor nightly; PLUMB/validator regression suites for worldbuilding correctness; the `gameplay-pie-and-testing` job-enveloped `test_run` and `pie_run_scenario` as the in-engine assertion layer; `networking-replication` single-process convergence asserts.
- **Packaging:** prebuilt binaries; `EngineVersion` widened beyond the pinned 5.7.0; engine-private-header includes removed (or non-marketplace status honestly documented); Fab metadata; auto-generated Epic-compatible `.mcp.json`.
- **Docs-as-a-tool:** indexed UE-API + PLUMB docs exposed as a queryable tool (stealing from Houdini/remiphilippe). This both serves agents at runtime and *is* the user documentation. Pair with `get_*_status` gating probes and paginated outputs everywhere.
- **Security:** wire the `auth` field and scopes (loopback no-auth is Epic's documented weakness — we should not copy it); DPAPI key vault; idempotency-gated retries.
- **Source control** (`source-control` domain): unattended agents must not leave a team's depot dirty/locked — checkout/changelist/submit is a production gate, not a nicety.

---

## 7. Live-Editor Testing Strategy (validating tools at scale)

You cannot unit-test 290 tools against a mocked editor and call it production-ready — UE Python coverage is version-variable and reflection is the whole point. The strategy validates against a *real running editor*, at scale, with the moats as the test oracle.

1. **The harness is itself a Hayba client.** A nightly job spins a headless/automation UE instance, connects over TCP, and drives the catalog. Reuse the `gameplay-pie-and-testing` job-envelope and `editor-introspection` `editor_state`/`wait_for_compile`/`mcp_health` gating probes as the readiness gates so the harness never races the editor.
2. **Inspection-first is the test contract.** Every write domain ships a paired `*_inspect`/`*_brief` read (the global rule). The harness pattern is **inspect → mutate → inspect → assert delta**. This is generic across all domains and falls straight out of the descriptor metadata — the test for "does `actor_batch_set_property` work" is "read the property, set it, read it back, diff."
3. **PLUMB and the validator are the oracle for worldbuilding correctness.** A screenshot cannot tell you a mesh is manifold or a foliage scatter hits its density constraint. The harness routes `mesh-and-geometryscript` topology numbers, `foliage-and-scatter` density, `landscape` slope/height, and `water-system` water-below-terrain through `plumb_validate`/`validator_run` and asserts on the verdict, not on pixels.
4. **Vision diff for the things only eyes catch.** The one parameterized capture core powers before/after diff, orbit, and buffer-viz. The harness captures, diffs, and (where useful) runs CLIP/SAM checks — `lighting`, `materials-and-textures`, `pcg`, `sequencer` stills.
5. **Crash-resilience is tested by fault injection.** Deliberately feed handlers malformed input and oversized frames; assert the SEH guard recovers and the editor survives. This is the moat — it must have an explicit test, not a hope.
6. **Transaction integrity tests.** After any multi-step outcome-verb, assert that exactly one Ctrl+Z reverts the whole step and a second inspect confirms the original state — the named differentiator must be regression-guarded.
7. **Scale via the job registry + parallel remote editors.** Long ops (cook, render, GatherText, fracture-bake) run through `FHaybaAsyncJobRegistry` so the harness polls instead of blocking, and independent domains run against separate editor instances in parallel. **Hard rule from prior experience: never run two app-driving agents against the same editor instance/port — serialize per instance.**

---

## 8. Risks & Open Questions

- **Epic's baseline gravity.** The default front door is free and sanctioned. **Mitigation:** interop, not competition — speak `list_toolsets`/`describe_toolset`/`call_tool`, register specialized toolsets into Epic's Toolset Registry, emit Epic-compatible `.mcp.json`. We are the depth layer on top of their breadth front door, not a replacement for it. *Open question: how far does extending their registry go before we're locked to their release cadence?*
- **The scaffolding refactor slips.** If Phase 1 isn't clean, every later count is fiction and we drown in 7-file churn. **Mitigation:** Phases 0+1 are absolute gates; `pyTemplate` proves out on `mesh-and-geometryscript`/`editor-introspection` before the blitz scales.
- **Duplicate-wrapper inflation.** The critic found ~40+ near-duplicate verbs and ~16 capture verbs that *will* drift. **Mitigation:** cross-cutting seams (transaction, capture, validate, dry-run, console/cvar, reflection-search, material-assign) are mandated in Phase 1; domains call them, never re-declare. *Open question: do we expose one generic `object_inspect`/`object_get_property` that supersedes the per-domain `actor_get_property`/`mesh_inspect`/`material_instance_inspect`, accepting coarser tools, or keep fine-grained inspects and accept divergence risk?*
- **Engine ceilings invite dishonesty.** Niagara emitter/module graphs, ControlRig rig-graphs, BlendSpace sample-graphs, and MetaSound graph mutation are genuine UE Python limits. **Mitigation:** scope to template-instantiation + param packs and *flag the limitation in the tool itself* — the honesty invariant. The temptation to fake these is exactly what produced `blueprint_add_node`.
- **`python_run` on the game thread.** Because python handlers run on the game thread, no long subprocess can live in python — `build`/`cook`/`package`/`launch`/render/GatherText stay C++ behind the job registry. **Mitigation:** this is a fixed architectural rule, documented in the add-a-tool guide so no design agent routes a long op through `pyTemplate`.
- **Transient handle lifetime (commit 5f3cf96).** A script's UObject wrappers are dropped inside the SEH guard, so `mesh-and-geometryscript` DynamicMesh handles don't survive across separate `python_run` calls. **Mitigation:** the `geo_pipeline` ordered-op pattern (source→dest in ONE call); a rooted C++ scratch-mesh pool is the optional robust path. *Open question: do other domains (PCG attribute readback, physics sim handles) hit the same wall and need their own single-call pipeline verbs?*
- **`FScopedTransaction` doesn't capture everything.** Python asset edits, project-ini edits (`gameplay-framework-defaults`, `localization`), and PCG graph-asset edits sit outside UE's undo. **Mitigation:** explicit checkpoint/restore snapshots for those domains; be honest in the tool docs about what one Ctrl+Z does and doesn't cover.
- **Distribution vs engine-private headers.** Marketplace acceptance may require removing engine-private includes. **Open question:** do we refactor to public API (cost, possibly losing capability) or ship non-marketplace with honest documentation and direct binaries? Recommend the latter short-term, the former as a Phase 4+ cleanup.
- **Maintaining 290 tools across UE versions.** Pinned at 5.7.0 today; competitors span 5.0–5.8. **Open question:** what's the per-version test matrix budget, and do we gate version support on the live-editor harness passing per version rather than promising blanket compatibility we can't verify?

---

<a id="domain-1"></a>
## 1. actor-level-editing

*37 tools — P0:14 P1:13 P2:10 P3:0*

The actor domain today is a 4-tool stub (spawn/delete/list/transform) — thinner than every competitor's actor surface and missing the unglamorous core: introspection, selection, batch/mass edits, hierarchy, reflection property-set, and transactional bundling. Strategy: (1) ship an inspection-first read pair for every write (actor_inspect/find/get_selection) so the agent never edits blind; (2) collapse Roblox/Unity-style mass ops into a few outcome-verbs (batch_set_property, batch_transform, replace_class) each wrapped in ONE FScopedTransaction so a mass edit is one Ctrl+Z — the named differentiator vs Epic's per-call undo; (3) lean the moats by routing every mutation through PLUMB validate-before/after and a dry_run→commit preview, and tying actor_focus into the vision loop. Nearly all ship as python_run-backed pyTemplate descriptors (EditorActorSubsystem + reflection) with zero plugin rebuild; only the cross-call transaction seam and bounds-buffer pass want C++ for robustness. This takes the domain from 4 honest tools to ~32, surpassing GenOrca/Roblox actor coverage while keeping the safety depth none of them have.

| Tool | Pri | Module | Parity | Description / signature |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `actor_spawn` | P0 | python_run-backed TS (EditorActorSubsystem.spawn_actor_from_object/class) | Epic baseline / chongdashu / GenOrca have spawn | Place a new actor of a class/asset in the active level — the base creation verb; harden existing tool with transaction-wrap + non-idempotent retry guard — `(class_path\|asset_path, location?, rotation?, scale?, label?, folder?) -> {actor_id, label, transform}` |
| `actor_spawn_from_asset` | P0 | python_run-backed TS | Roblox insert_asset; GenOrca; net-new convenience over class_path | Spawn a StaticMesh/Blueprint/asset by content path without knowing its actor class — the ergonomic 'place this mesh' verb agents actually reach for — `(asset_path, location?, rotation?, scale?, label?, snap_to_floor?) -> {actor_id, transform}` |
| `actor_duplicate` | P0 | python_run-backed TS (EditorActorSubsystem.duplicate_actor) | Roblox clone_object/mass_duplicate/smart_duplicate; Unity | Duplicate one or many actors with an offset/array, preserving overrides — avoids respawn+reconfigure; the most-used authoring op — `(actor_ids[], offset?, count?, axis?) -> {new_actor_ids[]}` |
| `actor_delete` | P0 | python_run-backed TS | all competitors | Destroy actor(s) by id/selection/query — lifecycle teardown; harden with Plan-Mode gate + transaction-wrap — `(actor_ids[]\|query?) -> {deleted_count, deleted_ids[]}` |
| `actor_inspect` | P0 | python_run-backed TS (reflection walk) | Roblox get_instance_properties; Flop *_inspect pattern; net-new depth | Full read-back of one actor: transform, class, components, key UProperties, material slots, tags, attachment, bounds — the inspection-first pair for every write; agents must never edit blind — `(actor_id, include_components?, include_properties?) -> {class, transform, bounds, components[], material_slots[], tags[], parent, properties{}}` |
| `actor_list` | P0 | python_run-backed TS | Unity paginated hierarchy; all peers | Paginated enumeration of actors in the level by class/tag/folder — the cheap survey read; add limit/has_more/next_offset — `(class_filter?, tag?, folder?, limit=50, offset=0) -> {actors[], total, has_more, next_offset}` |
| `actor_find` | P0 | python_run-backed TS | Roblox search_objects/search_by_property; Unity | Query actors by predicate (name regex, class, tag, material, inside-bounds, property-equals) returning ids — the selection-driver and batch-target resolver — `(name_regex?, class?, tag?, material?, within_box?, property?, value?, limit=50, offset=0) -> {actor_ids[], total, has_more}` |
| `actor_select` | P0 | python_run-backed TS (EditorActorSubsystem.set_selected_level_actors) | Roblox get_selection (read only); net-new write selection | Set the editor selection from ids or an inline query — makes selection an addressable target for batch ops and viewport capture — `(actor_ids[]\|query?, additive?) -> {selected_ids[], count}` |
| `actor_get_selection` | P1 | python_run-backed TS | Roblox get_selection; Unity | Read the current editor selection (ids + labels + classes) — lets the agent operate on what the user clicked; closes the human-in-loop gap — `() -> {selected_ids[], details[]}` |
| `actor_transform` | P0 | python_run-backed TS | Roblox move_object; all peers | Set absolute location/rotation/scale of one actor — base move verb; existing tool, transaction-wrap — `(actor_id, location?, rotation?, scale?) -> {transform}` |
| `actor_transform_relative` | P1 | python_run-backed TS | net-new moat (additive, multi) | Apply a delta move/rotate/scale to actor(s) — additive nudge that avoids reading-then-writing absolute values; safer for batch — `(actor_ids[], delta_location?, delta_rotation?, scale_mult?, space?) -> {transforms[]}` |
| `actor_batch_transform` | P0 | python_run-backed TS | Roblox mass_set_property; net-new transactional batch | Apply one transform/delta across a selection or query in a single transaction — outcome-verb replacing N per-actor calls; one Ctrl+Z — `(query\|actor_ids[], set?\|delta?, dry_run?) -> {affected_count, before_after?[]}` |
| `actor_batch_set_property` | P0 | python_run-backed TS (FProperty import_text) | Roblox mass_set_property/bulk_set_attributes; net-new with dry_run | Reflection set of any UProperty across many actors in one transaction — the mass-edit workhorse (e.g. set every light intensity); dry_run shows the diff first — `(query\|actor_ids[], property_path, value, dry_run?) -> {affected_count, failures[], diff?[]}` |
| `actor_set_property` | P0 | python_run-backed TS | Roblox set_property/set_properties; net-new reflection depth | Set a single arbitrary UProperty (incl. nested struct/component path) on one actor via reflection — the universal typed write replacing hand-rolled python — `(actor_id, property_path, value) -> {ok, old_value, new_value}` |
| `actor_get_property` | P1 | python_run-backed TS | Roblox get_attribute/get_instance_properties | Read one UProperty (nested path) off an actor/component — the read half of set_property; used for verify and conditional logic — `(actor_id, property_path) -> {value, type}` |
| `actor_replace_class` | P1 | python_run-backed TS | net-new moat (Roblox lacks class-swap) | Find-replace actor class across a selection, transferring transform/tags/folder/overrides where possible — swap placeholders for final assets without re-placing — `(actor_ids[]\|query, new_class_path, transfer_transform=true, dry_run?) -> {replaced[], new_ids[], unmapped_props[]}` |
| `actor_assign_material` | P1 | python_run-backed TS | Roblox search_materials/set; GenOrca; net-new batch | Set a material override on slot(s) of actor(s) — batch material-assign verb; pairs with PLUMB sliver/profile binding — `(actor_ids[]\|query, material_path, slot_index?\|slot_name?) -> {assigned_count}` |
| `actor_batch_rename` | P2 | python_run-backed TS | Roblox rename_object (single); net-new batch pattern | Rename a selection by a pattern with auto-numbering tokens — World Outliner hygiene at scale in one transaction — `(actor_ids[]\|query, pattern, start_index?) -> {renamed[]}` |
| `actor_set_label` | P2 | python_run-backed TS | Roblox rename_object | Rename a single actor's display label — the simple outliner rename — `(actor_id, label) -> {label}` |
| `actor_set_tags` | P1 | python_run-backed TS | Roblox add_tag/remove_tag/get_tags | Add/remove gameplay tags (actor.Tags) on actor(s) — makes actors queryable and groupable for later batch ops — `(actor_ids[]\|query, add?[], remove?[]) -> {tags_by_actor{}}` |
| `actor_set_folder` | P2 | python_run-backed TS | Unity hierarchy; net-new for UE peers | Move actor(s) into a World Outliner folder path — organizational hygiene the agent needs to keep big scenes navigable — `(actor_ids[]\|query, folder_path) -> {moved_count}` |
| `actor_attach` | P1 | python_run-backed TS (attach_to_actor) | net-new moat (peers lack scene-graph attach) | Attach actor(s) to a parent actor (optionally to a socket), keeping or zeroing world transform — builds hierarchies without manual component fiddling — `(child_ids[], parent_id, socket?, keep_world=true) -> {attached[]}` |
| `actor_detach` | P2 | python_run-backed TS | net-new moat | Detach actor(s) from their parent, preserving world transform — the inverse of attach — `(actor_ids[]) -> {detached[]}` |
| `actor_group` | P2 | python_run-backed TS (AActorGroupingUtils) | net-new moat | Create an editor group (or ungroup) from a selection — single-handle manipulation of related actors — `(actor_ids[], ungroup?=false) -> {group_id?, members[]}` |
| `actor_align` | P1 | python_run-backed TS | net-new moat (DCC-style align/distribute) | Align/distribute/snap-many a selection to an axis, the grid, or a reference actor's pivot/bounds — outcome-verb for layout cleanup — `(actor_ids[], mode: 'align'\|'distribute'\|'snap_grid', axis, reference?, grid_size?, dry_run?) -> {moved[]}` |
| `actor_snap_to_floor` | P1 | python_run-backed TS (line trace via EditorActorSubsystem) | net-new moat | Raycast down (or along a direction) and drop actor(s) onto underlying geometry/landscape — kills the floating/buried-asset problem after spawn — `(actor_ids[]\|query, direction?='down', align_to_normal?=false, offset?) -> {snapped[], missed[]}` |
| `actor_snap_to_socket` | P2 | python_run-backed TS | net-new moat | Snap/attach an actor onto a named socket of another actor's mesh — precise placement (e.g. prop into a hand/wall socket) — `(actor_id, target_id, socket_name) -> {transform}` |
| `actor_set_mobility` | P2 | python_run-backed TS | net-new moat | Set Static/Stationary/Movable mobility on actor(s) — gates lighting bake/physics; a common correctness fix agents otherwise miss — `(actor_ids[]\|query, mobility) -> {set_count}` |
| `actor_set_visibility` | P2 | python_run-backed TS | net-new moat | Toggle hidden-in-editor / hidden-in-game on actor(s) — staging and screenshot isolation for the vision loop — `(actor_ids[]\|query, editor_hidden?, game_hidden?) -> {set_count}` |
| `actor_add_component` | P2 | python_run-backed TS | net-new moat (peers stop at actor level) | Add a component (mesh/light/collision/etc.) to an actor instance and set its initial props — compose actors without a Blueprint — `(actor_id, component_class, name?, properties?) -> {component_id}` |
| `actor_bounds` | P1 | python_run-backed TS | net-new moat | Return world/local bounding box, pivot, and pairwise distance for actor(s) — the measure/verify read that drives align, snap, and auto-frame — `(actor_ids[], pairwise?=false) -> {bounds[], origin[], extent[], distances?}` |
| `actor_focus` | P1 | python_run-backed TS + editor_capture_viewport | Roblox capture_screenshot; extends Hayba vision moat | Select actor(s), frame the editor camera on their bounds, and (optionally) return a viewport PNG — ties actor edits into the vision feedback loop for before/after verification — `(actor_ids[]\|query, capture?=true) -> {framed_count, image?}` |
| `actor_transaction_begin` | P0 | C++ HaybaMCPToolkit (FScopedTransaction via HaybaGameThread::RunSync seam) | net-new moat (Epic = per-call undo only) | Open a named transaction so multiple subsequent actor edits collapse into ONE undo — the explicit multi-step bundling differentiator vs Epic's per-call undo — `(label) -> {transaction_id}` |
| `actor_transaction_commit` | P0 | C++ HaybaMCPToolkit | net-new moat | Close the open transaction, finalizing the bundle as a single undoable step (or cancel to roll back) — completes the run_tool_script→one-Ctrl+Z story — `(transaction_id, cancel?=false) -> {ok, undo_label}` |
| `actor_undo` | P1 | C++ HaybaMCPToolkit (GEditor->UndoTransaction) | Roblox undo; Unity undo/redo | Trigger editor Undo (optionally N steps) and report what reverted — recover from a bad batch without leaving the agent loop — `(steps?=1) -> {undone[], remaining_undo}` |
| `actor_redo` | P2 | C++ HaybaMCPToolkit | Roblox redo; Unity | Trigger editor Redo (N steps) — symmetric recovery — `(steps?=1) -> {redone[]}` |
| `actor_verify` | P1 | python_run-backed TS (+ PLUMB validator hook) | Flop pie_test assertions; Roblox compare_instances; PLUMB moat | Assert that actors match an expected spec (transform/property/material/tag) and return per-field pass/fail — the post-mutate read-back that turns guesses into closed-loop edits — `(actor_ids[], expect{}) -> {pass, mismatches[]}` |

**Dependencies:** EditorActorSubsystem / UnrealEditorSubsystem / LevelEditorSubsystem (UE Python Editor-Scripting) — backs ~30 of the tools; Hardened python_run handler (SEH + security gate + dangling-delegate gate) — the dispatch path for every pyTemplate descriptor; Theme 1 add-a-tool refactor: pyTemplate factory + single ToolDescriptor + auto-collected sidecar — required before bulk-adding these as first-class tools without 7-file churn; tool-executor.ts executeCommand transport (existing) — actor_spawn/delete/list/transform already use it; align timeouts + gate blind retry to idempotent ops (spawn/duplicate/delete are NOT idempotent); C++ HaybaMCPToolkit + HaybaGameThread::RunSync seam + FScopedTransaction — required for the cross-call transaction_begin/commit/undo/redo (Python ScopedEditorTransaction cannot span separate MCP calls); PLUMB validator hooks (validator_run / plumb_validate) — for actor_verify and validate-before/after-mutate on batch ops; editor_capture_viewport / render_camera (vision sidecar) — for actor_focus before/after capture; Plan-Mode gate (bPlanApproved) — gates destructive verbs (delete, replace_class, batch_set_property); FProperty/Reflection import_text coercion path — backs set_property / batch_set_property / get_property typed read-write

**Risks:** actor_id stability: content-path vs FGuid/label can drift across undo/redo, duplicate, and PIE — must pick a stable handle (level-relative path + guid) or batch ops target the wrong actors after a transaction; Reflection set_property type coercion: import_text on structs/enums/object-refs/arrays is brittle; silent no-ops or partial writes — every set must read back (hence actor_verify) and report per-field failures; Cross-call transactions: Python ScopedEditorTransaction is per-call only; spanning MCP calls needs the C++ seam, and a left-open transaction can swallow unrelated user edits into one undo — must auto-timeout/auto-commit and surface open-transaction state; Batch ops on large selections block the game thread (same class as the build_*/test_run freeze) — cap selection size, paginate, or route through a job-envelope; dry_run must not also stall; Non-idempotent retry: the transport's blind retry can double-spawn/double-delete — these verbs must be excluded from auto-retry; actor_replace_class loses component-level/instanced overrides not expressible on the new class — must report unmapped properties, not silently drop them; snap_to_floor / line traces hit invisible collision or nothing (no collision geometry, Nanite) — report missed actors rather than leaving them at origin; Editor groups / attach world-transform preservation differ between EditorActorSubsystem versions and UE 5.7→5.8 — pin and test per engine version

**Live-editor test plan:** "On a live UE editor with a scratch level: (1) actor_spawn_from_asset a cube → actor_inspect confirms transform/components. (2) actor_duplicate count=5 along X → actor_list paginated shows 6, has_more correct. (3) actor_select by class then actor_batch_set_property on a light-intensity-style prop with dry_run=true → confirm diff has 0 mutations, then commit and actor_verify each matches expected. (4) actor_transaction_begin → run batch_transform + assign_material + set_tags → transaction_commit → press Ctrl+Z ONCE in-editor and confirm ALL three revert together (the core differentiator), then actor_redo restores. (5) actor_align distribute on the 6 copies → actor_bounds pairwise confirms equal spacing. (6) actor_snap_to_floor over a landscape → snapped[] all sit on surface, missed[] empty; verify with render_camera. (7) actor_replace_class cube→a Blueprint actor → unmapped_props reported, transform transferred. (8) actor_attach two actors to a parent, move parent via actor_transform, actor_inspect children show world transform preserved. (9) actor_focus on the group with capture=true → returns a framed viewport PNG. (10) actor_delete with Plan-Mode unapproved → blocked; approve → deleted_count correct, actor_undo restores. Cross-check every read tool (inspect/find/get_property/bounds/get_selection) returns the same values the writes claimed; run validator_run before/after the batch to confirm no constraint regressions."


<a id="domain-2"></a>
## 2. mesh-and-geometryscript

*36 tools — P0:6 P1:16 P2:9 P3:5*

UE's GeometryScript library (UGeometryScriptLibrary_* operating on UDynamicMesh) is almost entirely BlueprintCallable and therefore reachable from python_run with zero plugin rebuild, so the whole procedural-mesh authoring loop ships as pyTemplate descriptors over the hardened python_run handler. The spine is inspect (existing C++ mesh_extract/topology readback, just needs first-class wrappers) -> edit (GeometryScript ops) -> validate (route topology numbers through PLUMB pass/fail) -> bake (CopyMeshToStaticMesh asset commit). Because commit 5f3cf96 drops a script's UObject wrappers inside the SEH guard, transient DynamicMesh handles do NOT survive across separate python_run calls, so the P0 vehicle is geo_pipeline (an ordered op list run source->dest in ONE call) plus atomic source->dest ops; a rooted C++ scratch-mesh pool is the optional robust path. Moats pressed: inspection-first paired reads, dry-run-before-commit (only world_generate had it), PLUMB quantified topology validation (manifold/winding/holes verdicts a screenshot cannot give), and transaction-wrapped single-undo baking. This out-tools every competitor's mesh surface; none validate topology numerically or dry-run mesh edits.</summary>
<parameter name="dependencies">["GeometryScripting + GeometryFramework UE plugins enabled (UGeometryScriptLibrary_* + UDynamicMesh/UDynamicMeshComponent)", "Existing C++ FHaybaMCPStaticMeshHandler (mesh_extract/mesh_topology_stats/mesh_list_dynamic) - wrap as first-class, do not reimplement", "Hardened python_run handler (SEH + 3-tier gate + dangling-delegate gate) as the pyTemplate dispatch substrate", "unreal.EditorStaticMeshLibrary / StaticMeshEditorSubsystem for Nanite/LOD/collision/lightmap authoring", "PLUMB stack (plumb_validate, plumb_profile_bake, plumb_socket_add) for the topology-validation and socket moats", "Add-a-tool pyTemplate factory (Theme 1, Section 5) - these descriptors are its first heavy customer", "Cross-cutting Transaction/undo C++ seam (Theme 4C) for single-Ctrl+Z bake bundling", "Vision loop (render_camera/editor_capture_viewport) for after-bake visual verify"]

| Tool | Pri | Module | Parity | Description / signature |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `mesh_inspect` | P0 | python_run-backed TS (or thin wrapper over existing C++ FHaybaMCPStaticMeshHandler::MeshExtract) | net-new moat (numeric topology verdict; competitors only screenshot) | First-class wrapper over the C++ mesh_extract: numeric geometry + topology readback (welds, winding, holes, components, surface area, volume, genus) a screenshot cannot confirm; the inspection-first read for every write op. — `(actor_label?\|asset_path?, lod?, component_index?, include_triangles?, include_positions?, include_normals?, max_elements?, bbox_min?, bbox_max?) -> {source, counts, topology, bounds, triangles?, vertices?, face_normals?, truncated}` |
| `mesh_list` | P0 | python_run-backed TS (wraps C++ MeshListDynamic) | net-new (wraps existing mesh_list_dynamic; Roblox get_descendants is closest) | Enumerate every UDynamicMeshComponent (and optionally StaticMeshActors) in the level with tri/vert/material counts; the discovery read for batch ops and PCG/grammar output inspection. — `(label_contains?, include_static?, limit?, offset?) -> {meshes:[{actor_label, component, triangles, vertices, num_materials, empty}], count, total_triangles, has_more, next_offset}` |
| `staticmesh_brief` | P1 | python_run-backed TS | net-new moat (deepest single-asset readback in any UE MCP) | Asset-level readback for a UStaticMesh: LOD count + screen sizes, Nanite enabled/settings, collision setup, UV channel count + lightmap UV index/resolution, material slots, bounds; the inspection-first read before any asset authoring op. — `(asset_path) -> {lods:[{index, triangles, screen_size, reduction}], nanite:{enabled, settings}, collision:{simple_prims, complex_as_simple, convex_count}, uv_channels, lightmap_index, lightmap_resolution, material_slots:[{slot, material_path}], bounds, nanite_fallback_pct}` |
| `mesh_validate` | P0 | python_run-backed TS + PLUMB validator stack | net-new moat (PLUMB quantified validation; beyond Flop auto-repair / Houdini graph-intelligence) | Topology health verdict: runs mesh_extract stats then routes through PLUMB to return green/red with an unsat-core (which check failed: non-manifold, inverted winding, open boundary, degenerate, multi-component); the validate-before/after-mutate gate. — `(actor_label?\|asset_path?, require_closed?, require_single_component?, max_boundary_edges?, profile?) -> {pass, checks:[{rule, value, threshold, pass}], unsat_core:[], plumb_profile?}` |
| `geo_pipeline` | P0 | python_run-backed TS (single-call orchestrator over UGeometryScriptLibrary_*) | net-new moat (no UE MCP composes/validates a mesh op chain) | Run an ordered list of GeometryScript ops source->dest in ONE python_run call (no cross-call handle GC risk); the P0 vehicle for composed procedural-mesh authoring with one transaction and a final validate. — `(source:{actor_label\|asset_path\|primitive}, ops:[{op, params}], dest:{actor_label\|new_asset_path}, dry_run?, validate?) -> {ok, ops_applied, before_stats, after_stats, validate?, dest_path}` |
| `geo_dry_run` | P0 | python_run-backed TS | net-new moat (only Flop bp_dry_run is analogous, not for meshes) | Preview a geo_pipeline: apply ops to a transient mesh and return predicted topology stats WITHOUT writing any asset or component; generalizes the dry_run->commit pattern beyond world_generate. — `(source, ops:[{op, params}]) -> {predicted_stats, predicted_validate, delta:{tris, verts, components}, warnings:[]}` |
| `geo_bake_to_staticmesh` | P0 | python_run-backed TS (UGeometryScriptLibrary_StaticMeshFunctions::CopyMeshToStaticMesh) | net-new (Blender import-after-gen is closest; none bake DynamicMesh->StaticMesh) | Commit a transient/dynamic mesh to a new or overwritten UStaticMesh asset (CopyMeshToStaticMesh) with Nanite/collision/lightmap/material options; the deliverable that turns procedural geometry into a usable asset. — `(source:{actor_label\|primitive\|pipeline}, dest_asset_path, nanite?, collision?, generate_lightmap_uvs?, recompute_normals?, overwrite?, dry_run?) -> {ok, asset_path, counts, topology, overwrote}` |
| `geo_apply_to_actor` | P1 | python_run-backed TS | parity with Aura live BP edit (mesh equivalent) | Write a transient/edited mesh back onto an actor's UDynamicMeshComponent for live preview without baking an asset; the fast iterate loop, transaction-wrapped for undo. — `(actor_label, source:{primitive\|pipeline\|asset_path}, component_index?, transactional?) -> {ok, counts, topology}` |
| `geo_primitive_new` | P1 | python_run-backed TS (UGeometryScriptLibrary_MeshPrimitiveFunctions) | parity Blender/Maya parametric prefab generators | Generate a parametric primitive DynamicMesh (box/sphere/cylinder/cone/torus/disc/stairs/grid) as a pipeline source; the cheapest content-creation entry point. — `(shape:Literal, dimensions?, subdivisions?, transform?) -> {source_ref, counts, bounds}` |
| `geo_from_asset` | P1 | python_run-backed TS (CopyMeshFromStaticMesh) | net-new | Load a UStaticMesh (chosen LOD) into a transient DynamicMesh as a pipeline source for editing; the read side of asset->edit->rebake. — `(asset_path, lod?) -> {source_ref, counts, topology}` |
| `geo_from_actor` | P1 | python_run-backed TS (CopyMeshFromComponent) | net-new | Copy an actor's static or dynamic mesh into a transient DynamicMesh source (world or local space) for non-destructive editing. — `(actor_label, component_index?, world_space?) -> {source_ref, counts, topology}` |
| `geo_boolean` | P1 | python_run-backed TS (UGeometryScriptLibrary_MeshBooleanFunctions) | parity Blender boolean | CSG union/subtract/intersect between two mesh sources (or a source and a primitive); the workhorse hard-surface op, with mandatory post-op manifold check. — `(source_a, source_b, operation:Literal[union,subtract,intersect], dest?, validate?, dry_run?) -> {ok, after_stats, validate}` |
| `geo_transform` | P1 | python_run-backed TS (MeshTransformFunctions) | parity (geometry-space, unlike actor_transform) | Translate/rotate/scale/uniform-scale a mesh or a sub-selection of triangles (by group/material/bbox); pivot-aware geometry transform distinct from actor transform. — `(source, translate?, rotate?, scale?, pivot?, selection?, dest?) -> {ok, bounds}` |
| `geo_remesh` | P1 | python_run-backed TS (MeshRemeshFunctions) | parity Houdini/Blender remesh | Uniform/adaptive remesh to a target edge length or triangle budget while preserving boundaries/UV seams; clean topology before simplify or for even shading. — `(source, target_edge_length?\|target_tri_count?, iterations?, preserve_uvs?, dest?, validate?) -> {ok, after_stats}` |
| `geo_simplify` | P1 | python_run-backed TS (MeshSimplifyFunctions) | parity Blender decimate | Decimate a mesh to a target tri count / percentage / geometric tolerance; the LOD-prep and budget-fit op, validated against a deviation threshold. — `(source, target:{tri_count\|percent\|tolerance}, preserve_boundary?, dest?, validate?) -> {ok, before_tris, after_tris, max_deviation}` |
| `geo_normals_recompute` | P1 | python_run-backed TS (MeshNormalsFunctions) | parity | Recompute per-vertex/per-face normals and tangents with hard/soft edges by angle threshold or polygroup; fixes the most common shading artifact after edits. — `(source, mode:Literal[by_angle,hard,soft,per_group], angle_threshold?, recompute_tangents?, dest?) -> {ok, split_normals}` |
| `geo_uv_project` | P1 | python_run-backed TS (MeshUVFunctions) + PLUMB Box-UV | net-new moat (PLUMB Box-UV node already shipped; productized as a tool) | Planar/box/cylinder/sphere UV projection onto a channel; the deterministic UV op, reuses the shipped PLUMB Box-UV world-space grid logic for swept/bonded shells. — `(source, mode:Literal[planar,box,cylinder,sphere], uv_channel?, world_space?, tile_size?, selection?, dest?) -> {ok, uv_channel, bounds_uv}` |
| `geo_weld` | P1 | python_run-backed TS (MeshBasicEditFunctions/Repair) | parity Blender merge-by-distance | Merge coincident vertices within a tolerance and optionally fix winding; the cleanup that turns PCG/boolean output from non-manifold to manifold. — `(source, tolerance?, fix_winding?, dest?, validate?) -> {ok, merged_verts, after_stats}` |
| `geo_fill_holes` | P2 | python_run-backed TS (MeshRepairFunctions) | parity | Close open boundary loops (flat/smooth/minimal fill) to make a shell watertight; required before voxel/Nanite/collision on PCG grammar shells. — `(source, method:Literal[minimal,flat,smooth], max_hole_edges?, dest?, validate?) -> {ok, holes_filled, remaining_boundary_loops}` |
| `geo_extrude` | P2 | python_run-backed TS (MeshModelingFunctions) | parity Blender extrude | Extrude whole mesh or a face/group selection along normals or a vector (with shell thickness); core hard-surface modeling op for swept profiles. — `(source, distance, direction?:Literal[normal,vector], vector?, selection?, solidify?, dest?) -> {ok, after_stats}` |
| `geo_sweep_revolve` | P2 | python_run-backed TS (MeshPrimitiveFunctions revolve/sweep) + PLUMB | net-new moat (PLUMB swept-shell pipeline) | Sweep a 2D profile along a spline/path or revolve it around an axis to generate shells; the PLUMB swept/bonded-shell worldbuilding generator productized. — `(profile:{points\|primitive}, mode:Literal[sweep,revolve], path?:{spline_actor\|points}, axis?, steps?, cap_ends?, dest?, validate?) -> {ok, after_stats}` |
| `geo_mirror` | P2 | python_run-backed TS (MeshTransformFunctions) | parity Blender mirror | Mirror a mesh across a plane with optional weld-on-seam; symmetry authoring without manual duplicate+flip. — `(source, plane:Literal[x,y,z,custom], plane_origin?, plane_normal?, weld_seam?, dest?) -> {ok, after_stats}` |
| `geo_displace` | P2 | python_run-backed TS (MeshDeformFunctions) | parity Houdini/Gaea displace | Displace vertices by Perlin/texture/normal-direction with a mask; adds surface detail to terrain/rock/organic shells procedurally. — `(source, mode:Literal[perlin,texture,normal], intensity, frequency?, texture_path?, mask?, dest?) -> {ok, max_displacement}` |
| `geo_voxel` | P2 | python_run-backed TS (MeshVoxelFunctions) | parity Houdini VDB | Voxel/morphological remesh (offset, close, open, mesh-to-watertight) to guarantee a manifold closed solid; the robust repair path for messy boolean/PCG output. — `(source, operation:Literal[remesh,offset,close,open], voxel_size, offset_distance?, dest?, validate?) -> {ok, after_stats, watertight}` |
| `geo_subdivide` | P3 | python_run-backed TS (MeshSubdivideFunctions) | parity | PN/Loop/Catmull-Clark subdivision to a level; smooths low-poly cages for organic forms. — `(source, method:Literal[pn,loop,catmull], iterations, dest?, validate?) -> {ok, after_stats}` |
| `geo_smooth` | P3 | python_run-backed TS (MeshDeformFunctions) | parity | Laplacian/Taubin smoothing with iterations and boundary preservation; denoise scanned or displaced meshes. — `(source, method:Literal[laplacian,taubin], iterations, strength?, preserve_boundary?, dest?) -> {ok}` |
| `geo_uv_unwrap` | P3 | python_run-backed TS (MeshUVFunctions) | parity Blender smart-UV / RizomUV | Auto-unwrap UVs via PatchBuilder/XAtlas with island packing; full UV generation for textured assets (slow/nondeterministic, capped tri count). — `(source, method:Literal[patch_builder,xatlas], uv_channel?, island_padding?, resolution?, dest?) -> {ok, island_count, packing_efficiency}` |
| `staticmesh_set_nanite` | P1 | python_run-backed TS (StaticMeshEditorSubsystem) | net-new (no UE MCP exposes Nanite) | Enable/disable Nanite and set fallback/relative-error/position-precision on a UStaticMesh, then build; the single easiest high-value asset win. — `(asset_path, enabled, fallback_percent?, relative_error?, position_precision?, dry_run?) -> {ok, nanite_settings, fallback_tris}` |
| `staticmesh_generate_lods` | P1 | python_run-backed TS (EditorStaticMeshLibrary) | parity (rare; remiphilippe build-only) | Auto-generate reduction LODs (count, per-LOD percent/screen-size) on a UStaticMesh; performance authoring without the editor LOD UI. — `(asset_path, num_lods, reduction_percents?, screen_sizes?, dry_run?) -> {ok, lods:[{index, triangles, screen_size}]}` |
| `staticmesh_collision_set` | P1 | python_run-backed TS (EditorStaticMeshLibrary) | net-new | Set simple collision (box/sphere/capsule/convex-decomposition via Chaos) or complex-as-simple on a UStaticMesh; gameplay-ready geometry. — `(asset_path, type:Literal[box,sphere,capsule,convex,complex_as_simple,none], convex_hull_count?, hull_verts?, dry_run?) -> {ok, prim_count}` |
| `staticmesh_setup_lightmap` | P2 | python_run-backed TS (EditorStaticMeshLibrary) | net-new | Generate lightmap UVs into a channel and set lightmap UV index + resolution; fixes baked-lighting artifacts on procedural assets. — `(asset_path, lightmap_index?, resolution?, generate_uvs?, source_uv?, dry_run?) -> {ok, lightmap_index, resolution}` |
| `staticmesh_socket_add` | P2 | python_run-backed TS + PLUMB socket_add | net-new moat (PLUMB sockets) | Add/list named sockets (transform + tag) on a UStaticMesh for attachment; reuses the PLUMB socket grammar so generated shells expose bind points. — `(asset_path, action:Literal[add,list,remove], socket_name?, location?, rotation?, scale?, tag?) -> {ok, sockets:[{name, transform, tag}]}` |
| `staticmesh_assign_material` | P2 | python_run-backed TS | parity Roblox set_property material | Set/swap material on a slot index (or all slots) of a UStaticMesh or per-instance on an actor; closes the texture/material loop after baking geometry. — `(asset_path\|actor_label, slot_index?\|all?, material_path) -> {ok, slots:[{slot, material_path}]}` |
| `mesh_batch_apply` | P1 | python_run-backed TS | parity Roblox mass_set_property/Unity batch wiring (net-new for meshes) | Apply a geo op (or short pipeline) across many actors/assets selected by query/class/tag, returning per-target verdicts; outcome-verb batch op for grammar/PCG output cleanup. — `(targets:{selection\|class\|tag\|label_contains}, ops:[{op, params}], dest_mode:Literal[in_place,new_assets], dry_run?, validate_each?) -> {applied, failed, results:[{target, ok, after_stats, validate}], has_more, next_offset}` |
| `mesh_compare` | P3 | python_run-backed TS (MeshComparisonFunctions) | net-new moat (no UE MCP diffs geometry) | Diff two meshes (Hausdorff distance, tri/vert delta, volume/area delta, topology delta); before/after verification and regression check after edits or LOD gen. — `(source_a, source_b, sample_count?) -> {hausdorff, mean_deviation, tri_delta, vert_delta, volume_delta, area_delta, topology_delta}` |
| `geo_scratch_pool` | P3 | C++: HaybaMCPToolkit (rooted UDynamicMesh pool) - python_run cannot persist handles safely | net-new (handle session like remote DCC) | Optional rooted-handle lifecycle: new/list/free named transient DynamicMesh handles that survive across calls; only robust if backed by a C++ rooted pool (python_run GCs wrappers), so flagged advanced. — `(action:Literal[new,list,free,free_all], handle?, source?) -> {handles:[{handle, counts, age}]}` |

**Risks:** python_run UObject GC (commit 5f3cf96): transient DynamicMesh wrappers are dropped after each call, so cross-call geo_mesh handles are unreliable - lead with single-call geo_pipeline; only ship the handle pool if backed by a rooted C++ pool in HaybaMCPToolkit; GeometryScripting plugin may be disabled in a target project; every geo_* tool must probe and return an actionable 'enable GeometryScripting' error code, not a python stack trace; CopyMeshToStaticMesh / build on a large mesh can approach python_run timeout; heavy bakes (Nanite build, LOD gen, convex decomposition) may need the job-envelope async path (Theme 0) rather than a sync call; Mutating an actor's live UDynamicMeshComponent without FScopedTransaction yields no undo; bake/apply ops must wrap in the C++ transaction seam (4C) or be flagged non-undoable; Boolean/voxel/remesh can silently produce non-manifold or inverted-winding output; mandatory post-op mesh_validate prevents shipping broken geometry; Overwriting an existing UStaticMesh asset breaks references and is destructive - must respect Plan-Mode gate and default to dry_run + new-asset-path; UV unwrap (XAtlas/PatchBuilder) is nondeterministic and slow; treat as P2 and cap input tri count

**Live-editor test plan:** On a live editor with GeometryScripting enabled: (1) Spawn or pick a PCG grammar shell actor; run mesh_inspect + mesh_list to confirm the C++ readback surfaces tris/verts/topology. (2) geo_primitive_new(box) -> geo_bake_to_staticmesh(new path); reopen the asset and mesh_inspect it to confirm counts and a closed manifold (non_manifold_vertices==0, is_closed). (3) geo_dry_run a boolean-subtract pipeline and confirm predicted topology stats return WITHOUT creating an asset; then geo_pipeline the same ops with commit and diff predicted vs actual via mesh_validate. (4) geo_remesh + geo_simplify to a target tri count, verify mesh_validate reports manifold + the tri budget hit. (5) staticmesh_set_nanite + staticmesh_generate_lods, then staticmesh_brief to read back nanite flag, LOD count, screen sizes, collision, lightmap UV index. (6) geo_uv_project(box) then mesh_inspect UV-channel report; render_camera before/after for visual confirm. (7) mesh_batch_apply a normals-recompute across N selected actors; confirm count and per-actor verdicts. (8) Run an intentionally broken op (boolean producing holes) and confirm mesh_validate returns red with a non-zero boundary_loops unsat-core. (9) Bake, then editor undo (Ctrl+Z) once, confirm the whole pipeline reverts as a single transaction. (10) Disable GeometryScripting and confirm every geo_* tool returns the actionable enable-plugin error code.


<a id="domain-3"></a>
## 3. materials-and-textures

*34 tools — P0:9 P1:15 P2:8 P3:2*

The graph-authoring half of materials is already strong (~20 first-class tools), but the author LOOP is missing: no way to read back what parameters/static-switches/nodes a material exposes, no batch/multi-select, no slot-aware apply (current material_apply touches only a single StaticMeshComponent), no texture import/export/channel-pack, no Material Parameter Collections, and no visual verify. This adds ~34 tools on those gaps, almost all python_run-backed pyTemplate descriptors (zero plugin rebuild) per the "no rebuild by default" rule, reserving C++ only for hot-path pixel work. It leans on three moats: PLUMB-style convention validation (texture_audit / fix_settings_by_convention enforce correct sRGB-on-normals and compression discipline), the vision loop (material_thumbnail / preview_on_mesh return PNG content blocks), and dry-run/inspection-first safety (every write domain ships a paired *_inspect read and a dry-run batch planner). Every mutation is paginated, flat-arg, and routed through the hardened python_run handler so it is schema-validated, journaled, and transaction-wrappable without per-tool 7-file churn.

| Tool | Pri | Module | Parity | Description / signature |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `material_param_list` | P0 | python_run-backed TS (pyTemplate; MaterialEditingLibrary.get_*_parameter_names/value) | GenOrca has partial material-graph readback; full param+override inspect is net-new moat | List every parameter (scalar/vector/texture/static-switch) of a material or instance with current value, parent default, group, and override flag; the missing read that lets an agent know what is settable before it sets. — `(path, include_inherited=true, limit=50, offset=0) -> {params:[{name,type,value,default,group,overridden}], has_more, next_offset}` |
| `material_instance_inspect` | P0 | python_run-backed TS (pyTemplate; MaterialInstanceConstant introspection) | Flop inspection-pattern parity | Full brief for one material instance constant: parent chain, overridden-vs-inherited params, static switches, quality/usage flags; the inspection-first pair for all instance writes. — `(instance_path) -> {parent, overrides:{...}, static_switches:{...}, usage_flags:[...], quality_overrides}` |
| `material_node_list` | P1 | C++ HaybaMCPMaterialHandler (extend material_get_info; UE Python graph walk is shallow) | chongdashu BP-graph parity, applied to material graph | Enumerate graph nodes (id, expression class, position, input/output connections) for a material or function; the paired read for the existing add/connect/delete node authoring tools. — `(material_path\|function_path, limit=50, offset=0) -> {nodes:[{id,class,pos,inputs,outputs}], has_more, next_offset}` |
| `material_list_textures` | P1 | python_run-backed TS (pyTemplate; AssetRegistry dependencies + texture-param walk) | net-new moat | Forward dependency: list textures sampled by a material/instance (with sample type and param name); needed to reason about memory and channel usage. — `(path) -> {textures:[{path,param_name,sampler_type}]}` |
| `material_find_references` | P1 | python_run-backed TS (pyTemplate; EditorAssetLibrary.find_package_referencers) | Unity paginated-hierarchy parity | Reverse lookup: which assets/actors use this material or instance; the unglamorous safety check before delete/reparent. — `(path, scope=all\|level\|content, limit=50, offset=0) -> {referencers:[{path,kind}], has_more, next_offset}` |
| `texture_find_references` | P2 | python_run-backed TS (pyTemplate; AssetRegistry referencers) | net-new moat | Reverse lookup: which materials reference a texture; pairs with material_list_textures for full dependency graphing. — `(texture_path, limit=50, offset=0) -> {materials:[...], has_more, next_offset}` |
| `texture_audit` | P1 | python_run-backed TS (pyTemplate; metadata scan + validator rules) | net-new moat (PLUMB convention validation) | Scan textures (under a prefix) for convention violations: sRGB on normal maps, oversized/non-pow2, missing compression, wrong LOD group; a PLUMB-style closed-loop correctness report. — `(path_prefix, fix=false, limit=100, offset=0) -> {issues:[{path,rule,actual,expected}], summary, has_more, next_offset}` |
| `material_audit` | P2 | python_run-backed TS (pyTemplate; reuse material_stats + flag checks) | net-new moat (beyond Flop auto-repair rules) | Scan materials for cost/correctness issues: missing usage flags, two-sided/translucency cost, high instruction or sampler counts; report with machine codes. — `(path_prefix, limit=100, offset=0) -> {issues:[{path,rule,severity,detail}], has_more, next_offset}` |
| `material_set_params_bulk` | P0 | python_run-backed TS (pyTemplate; MaterialEditingLibrary.set_*_parameter_value loop) | ChiR24 batch-verb parity | Set many parameters (scalar/vector/texture mixed) on ONE instance in a single call; outcome-verb that replaces N material_set_param round-trips. — `(instance_path, params:{name:value,...}, dry_run=false) -> {applied:[...], changed:int, errors:[...]}` |
| `material_set_static_switch` | P0 | python_run-backed TS (pyTemplate; MaterialInstanceConstant static parameters; PR#250 deferred-compile pattern) | net-new (most peers omit static switches) | Set static-switch parameter(s) on a material instance (separate from scalar/vector path); deferred-compile to avoid game-thread shader stalls. — `(instance_path, switches:{name:bool,...}, defer_compile=true) -> {applied, recompiled:bool}` |
| `material_param_reset` | P1 | python_run-backed TS (pyTemplate; clear override arrays + post_edit_change) | net-new moat | Clear overridden parameters on an instance back to parent default (one name or all); restores inheritance cleanly. — `(instance_path, param_name=null, scope=scalar\|vector\|texture\|switch\|all) -> {reset:[...]}` |
| `material_instance_set_parent` | P2 | python_run-backed TS (pyTemplate; set_parent + reconcile) | net-new moat | Reparent a material instance constant to a new parent material, preserving compatible overrides; inspect-first safety report of dropped overrides. — `(instance_path, new_parent_path, dry_run=false) -> {dropped_overrides:[...], ok}` |
| `material_batch_set_param` | P0 | python_run-backed TS (pyTemplate; AssetRegistry filter + set loop) | Roblox mass_set_property parity | Set one parameter across many instances matched by path glob/query; the mass-edit verb for tuning a whole material family at once. — `(query\|paths[], param_name, value, dry_run=false, limit=200) -> {matched:int, applied:int, errors:[...]}` |
| `material_batch_create_instances` | P1 | python_run-backed TS (pyTemplate; create MIC + set params loop) | Maya parametric-prefab-generator parity | Create N material instance constants from one parent, each with per-instance param overrides; parametric variant generation in one call. — `(parent_path, dest_folder, variants:[{name,params}]) -> {created:[paths], errors:[...]}` |
| `material_apply_to_selection` | P0 | python_run-backed TS (pyTemplate; EditorActorSubsystem selection + set_material per component) | Unity batch-wiring parity | Assign a material to all selected actors, slot-aware and across ALL component types (Static/Skeletal/Instanced); fixes the StaticMesh-only limitation of material_apply. — `(material_path, slot=-1, component_filter=all) -> {actors:int, slots_set:int, skipped:[...]}` |
| `material_apply_batch` | P1 | python_run-backed TS (pyTemplate; actor query + set_material) | net-new moat | Assign a material to actors matched by class/tag/name query at a given slot index; outcome-verb for dressing many actors without manual selection. — `(material_path, query, slot=-1, dry_run=false, limit=500) -> {matched, applied, errors:[...]}` |
| `texture_batch_set_settings` | P0 | python_run-backed TS (pyTemplate; reuse texture_set_settings per asset) | Roblox bulk_set_attributes parity | Apply compression/lod-group/sRGB/mip settings to many textures matched by query in one call; the bulk-edit verb for fixing an imported set. — `(query\|paths[], properties:{...}, dry_run=false, limit=300) -> {matched, applied, errors:[...]}` |
| `texture_fix_settings_by_convention` | P1 | python_run-backed TS (pyTemplate; suffix map + texture_set_settings) | net-new moat (PLUMB convention language) | Auto-detect each texture's role from its suffix (_D/_N/_ORM/_M/_R/_AO) and apply the correct compression+sRGB+LOD group; PLUMB-style convention enforcement with dry-run plan first. — `(path_prefix, ruleset=default, dry_run=true, limit=300) -> {plan:[{path,role,changes}], applied:int}` |
| `texture_import` | P0 | python_run-backed TS (pyTemplate; AssetImportTask / Interchange) | Blender asset-import parity | Import an image file (PNG/TGA/EXR/HDR) into a Texture2D asset with settings applied at import; the entry point for external art. — `(source_file, dest_folder, name, properties={}, overwrite=false) -> {path, size, format}` |
| `texture_import_batch` | P1 | python_run-backed TS (pyTemplate; glob + AssetImportTask + convention) | net-new (extends Blender single-import) | Bulk-import all images in a source folder, auto-naming and auto-assigning settings by suffix convention; folder-to-content in one call. — `(source_dir, dest_folder, pattern=*, apply_convention=true) -> {imported:[paths], skipped:[...], errors:[...]}` |
| `texture_reimport` | P2 | python_run-backed TS (pyTemplate; reimport_asset) | net-new moat | Reimport a texture from its original source file on disk; refreshes edited source art without losing settings. — `(texture_path, new_source=null) -> {ok, source_used}` |
| `texture_export` | P1 | python_run-backed TS (pyTemplate; export_render_target / source-data write) | net-new moat | Export a Texture2D / render target to disk as PNG/EXR for inspection, round-trip, or channel-pack verification. — `(texture_path, out_file, format=png) -> {out_file, size}` |
| `texture_create_render_target` | P1 | python_run-backed TS (pyTemplate; create TextureRenderTarget2D) | net-new moat | Create a TextureRenderTarget2D asset (size/format) for SceneCapture, RVT, or channel-pack pipelines; a prerequisite primitive others depend on. — `(dest_path, width, height, format=RGBA8, srgb=false) -> {path}` |
| `texture_channel_pack` | P1 | python_run-backed TS (pyTemplate; pack material + draw_material_to_render_target -> static texture) | net-new moat | GPU-pack up to four grayscale source textures into one RGBA mask/ORM texture via draw_material_to_render_target; the high-value worldbuilding ORM-packing verb. — `(r_tex, g_tex, b_tex, a_tex, dest_path, size=auto) -> {path, channels_used}` |
| `texture_create_solid` | P2 | python_run-backed TS (pyTemplate; draw_material_to_render_target or pixel write) | net-new moat | Create a procedural placeholder texture (solid color / checker / gradient) without external art; unblocks material wiring before real textures exist. — `(dest_path, kind=solid\|checker\|gradient, color=[r,g,b,a], size=256) -> {path}` |
| `material_duplicate` | P2 | python_run-backed TS (pyTemplate; EditorAssetLibrary.duplicate_asset) | ChiR24 / Unity duplicate parity | Duplicate a material, instance, or material function to a new path; the safe-copy primitive before destructive edits. — `(source_path, dest_path) -> {path}` |
| `texture_duplicate` | P3 | python_run-backed TS (pyTemplate; duplicate_asset) | ChiR24 duplicate parity | Duplicate a texture asset to a new path, preserving settings; companion to material_duplicate. — `(source_path, dest_path) -> {path}` |
| `mpc_create` | P1 | python_run-backed TS (pyTemplate; create MaterialParameterCollection) | net-new moat | Create a Material Parameter Collection asset with named scalar/vector entries; the worldbuilding-global-parameter primitive (e.g. global wetness, season, time-of-day). — `(dest_path, scalars:[name], vectors:[name]) -> {path}` |
| `mpc_set` | P0 | python_run-backed TS (pyTemplate; MaterialParameterCollectionInstance set) | net-new moat | Set scalar/vector values on a Material Parameter Collection at runtime/edit-time; drives every material reading the collection in one call. — `(mpc_path, scalars:{name:val}, vectors:{name:[rgba]}) -> {applied:[...]}` |
| `mpc_inspect` | P2 | python_run-backed TS (pyTemplate; MPC introspection) | net-new moat | List a Material Parameter Collection's entries and current values; the inspection-first pair for mpc_set. — `(mpc_path) -> {scalars:{...}, vectors:{...}}` |
| `material_thumbnail` | P1 | MIX: python_run thumbnail render + render_camera; PNG content block | net-new moat (strongest-in-class vision loop) | Render a material's preview-sphere thumbnail to a PNG content block; the vision-loop verify so an agent can SEE the result of a param change. — `(path, size=256) -> {image: png content block}` |
| `material_preview_on_mesh` | P2 | MIX: python_run (show-only-list mesh, pre-warm shaders) + SceneCapture; PNG content blocks | net-new moat | Apply a material to an isolated preview mesh in a SceneCapture and return before/after PNGs; visual diff of a material on real geometry without disturbing the live world. — `(material_path, mesh=sphere\|cube\|plane\|asset_path, before_material=null) -> {before_png, after_png}` |
| `material_dry_run_batch` | P1 | python_run-backed TS (orchestration over the batch tools in read-only mode) | Flop bp_dry_run parity | Preview a batch of param/apply/setting mutations as a diff of exactly what would change, without committing; generalizes the world_generate dry_run->commit pattern to materials. — `(plan:[{tool,args}]) -> {diff:[{target,field,from,to}], conflicts:[...], commit_token}` |
| `texture_streaming_report` | P3 | python_run-backed TS (pyTemplate; texture resource size scan) | net-new moat | Report texture streaming-pool / memory usage and flag the biggest offenders by on-disk and in-memory size; the optimization read for budget work. — `(path_prefix=null, top=20) -> {total_mb, pool_mb, offenders:[{path,mb,dims}]}` |

**Dependencies:** UE Editor Scripting: unreal.MaterialEditingLibrary (get/set_*_parameter_value, get_*_parameter_names) for instance param read/write; unreal.MaterialInstanceConstant + MaterialInstanceEditorLibrary for static switches, reparent, override clearing; unreal.AssetImportTask / AssetToolsHelpers / InterchangeManager for texture_import(_batch)/reimport; unreal.EditorAssetLibrary (duplicate_asset, save_asset, find_package_referencers) + AssetRegistryHelpers for find_references / forward deps; unreal.RenderingLibrary.draw_material_to_render_target + TextureRenderTarget2D + export_render_target for channel-pack, preview_on_mesh, texture_export; unreal.MaterialParameterCollection(+Instance) APIs for the mpc_* tools; Existing infra: tool-executor.executeCommand + hardened python_run handler (SEH + 3-tier security gate) as the pyTemplate dispatch vehicle (Theme 1 #4); schema-registry + ToolDescriptor pipeline (Section 5); Vision: render_camera / editor_capture_viewport / SceneCapture world-pos spike for material_thumbnail + preview_on_mesh; C++ HaybaMCPTextureHandler / HaybaMCPMaterialHandler for the 2 hot-path readback tools (pixel histogram, instruction-count analyze) if Python proves too slow; PLUMB validator (validator_run / plumb_validate, convention/constraint language) for texture_audit + material_audit + fix_settings_by_convention rule sourcing
