| `umg_preview_capture` | P1 | python_run-backed TS + vision sidecar (SWidget render / thumbnail) | GenOrca vision overlay / Blender thumbnail | Render a thumbnail/preview image of the widget design and return it as a PNG content block — vision-loop verification of layout/style. — `(widget_blueprint_path, width?=512, height?=512) -> image content block + {ok}` |
| `umg_preview_in_pie` | P1 | MIX: python_run-backed TS + ue_legacy PIE handler | remiphilippe PIE / Flop pie_test_bp | Instantiate the UserWidget, add it to the PIE viewport, screenshot, and tear down — runtime add-to-viewport verify the catalog lacks. — `(widget_blueprint_path, hold_ms?=300) -> image content block + {ok, pie_active}` |
| `umg_validate` | P2 | TS (validator_run + UMG rule pack) | net-new moat (beyond Flop auto-repair) | Run a PLUMB/validator UI pass: off-screen / zero-size / overlapping widgets, low text-contrast, unbound dynamic text, accessibility flags — the validate-before/after-mutate moat for UI. — `(widget_blueprint_path, rules?) -> {issues:[{widget,code,severity,msg}], passed}` |
| `umg_transaction` | P1 | HaybaMCPToolkit C++ (FScopedTransaction seam, 4C) | net-new moat (vs Epic per-call undo) | Bundle a sequence of UMG edits into ONE undo (begin/commit/undo) via FScopedTransaction — named differentiator vs Epic's per-call undo. — `(action:'begin'\|'commit'\|'undo', label?) -> {ok, txn_id}` |

**Risks:** UE Python cannot reliably mutate WidgetTree structure (construct/reparent/rename widgets) — structural tools MUST be C++ in HaybaMCPUIHandler, not python_run; python attempts are flaky across 5.7/5.8; Existing handler MarksBlueprintModified but never compiles or saves — edits silently lost on editor restart; umg_compile + explicit asset save are P0 to make every other tool honest; UMG widget animation authoring and event/property bindings are genuine UE Python ceilings (like Niagara/ControlRig) — scope to template-instantiation + flag as engine limitation; do not over-promise; ApplySlotProps currently force-resets CanvasPanelSlot anchors to top-left and treats w/h as Right/Bottom offsets — naive anchored layouts break; umg_set_anchors must expose true min/max anchors + alignment; ConstructWidget name collisions and AddChild rejection (panel full / incompatible slot, e.g. Button accepts one child) need actionable error codes, not silent failure; Reflection-based set_property (ImportText_Direct) can partially-apply structs (FSlateBrush, FSlateColor, FSlateFontInfo) — needs typed convenience tools (set_brush/set_text/set_style) over raw reflection; Mutating a WBP that is open in the editor can desync the live designer; verify-after-mutate via inspect/read-back; compose_tree in one shot risks half-built trees on mid-spec failure — must run inside FScopedTransaction so a failure rolls back atomically

**Live-editor test plan:** Live-editor loop on a scratch path /Game/UI_MCPTest. 1) umg_widget_create WBP_Test (UserWidget) -> assert asset exists + RootCanvas root; reopen-safe via umg_compile then save. 2) umg_palette_list -> confirm Button/TextBlock/Image/VerticalBox + any custom UserWidget resolve. 3) umg_compose_dry_run a 6-widget spec (Canvas>VBox>{TextBlock,Button>TextBlock,Image}) -> expect ok with resolved classes, no mutation. 4) umg_compose_tree same spec -> umg_widget_inspect and assert hierarchy + slot types match (verify-after-mutate). 5) umg_set_anchors center-stretch on the VBox -> inspect slot offsets/anchors round-trip. 6) umg_set_text + umg_set_brush + umg_set_property(Visibility) -> umg_widget_get_properties read-back confirms each. 7) umg_batch_set_property color across all TextBlocks (class glob) -> read-back count. 8) umg_rename_widget + umg_reparent_widget + umg_remove_widget -> inspect confirms tree delta. 9) umg_transaction wrapping steps 5-7 then a single undo -> inspect confirms all three reverted in ONE Ctrl+Z. 10) umg_preview_capture -> returns PNG content block of the design; umg_preview_in_pie -> add to viewport in PIE + screenshot shows widget. 11) umg_validate -> flags an intentionally off-screen / zero-size / low-contrast widget. 12) Negative: umg_add_widget a 2nd child to a Button (single-child) -> expect actionable error code, not crash; confirm SEH/ProcessCommand keeps editor alive.


<a id="domain-21"></a>
## 21. Enhanced Input asset authoring

*33 tools — P0:8 P1:11 P2:10 P3:4*

Enhanced Input is a hard UE5 design-time gap: every competitor (and Hayba today) only does RUNTIME injection, while IA/IMC asset authoring is unautomated. Hayba already has three unwrapped C++ commands (input_create_action/mapping/add_mapping in HaybaMCPInputHandler) that are not first-class and not in the sidecar, so Step 1 is surfacing that tier-2 debt as proper TS tools; Step 2 fills the whole author loop (inspect, rebind, configure triggers/modifiers, validate, template) mostly via python_run with zero rebuilds. The load-bearing constraint: UInputMappingContext.map_key returns FEnhancedActionKeyMapping by VALUE in UE Python (it is a C++ reference), so trigger/modifier mutation must stay in C++ or rewrite the whole mappings TArray; creation, property-set, and all read-back are pure Python. We lean the crash-resilience, PLUMB-validation, and transactional-undo moats via inspection-first read tools, an input_validate conflict checker, dry-run on every batch op, and one-Ctrl+Z transaction bundling. Net-new moat: an end-to-end design-time to PIE-runtime input loop no other UE MCP closes.

| Tool | Pri | Module | Parity | Description / signature |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `input_action_create` | P0 | C++ HaybaMCPToolkit/HaybaMCPInputHandler (input_create_action exists) + thin TS wrapper + sidecar entry | net-new moat (only runtime injection exists across all competitors) | Create an Input Action asset with a value type (Boolean/Axis1D/2D/3D) plus optional consume/pause/description — the atom every binding references; surfaces the existing-but-unwrapped C++ command. — `(path, name, value_type:Literal[boolean\|axis1d\|axis2d\|axis3d], description?, consume_input?, trigger_when_paused?) -> {path, value_type}` |
| `input_context_create` | P0 | C++ HaybaMCPToolkit/HaybaMCPInputHandler (input_create_mapping) + TS wrapper | net-new moat | Create an empty Input Mapping Context asset — the container that binds actions to keys; wraps the existing unwrapped C++ command. — `(path, name) -> {path}` |
| `input_map_key` | P0 | C++ HaybaMCPToolkit/HaybaMCPInputHandler (input_add_mapping) + TS wrapper | net-new moat | Bind an action to a key inside an IMC, attaching triggers + modifiers in one call — the core authoring verb; must use the C++ reference path because Python's map_key returns the mapping by value. — `(context_path, action_path, key, triggers?:string[], modifiers?:string[]) -> {mapping_index, key, triggers[], modifiers[], unresolved_classes[]}` |
| `input_context_inspect` | P0 | python_run-backed TS | net-new moat | Read back every mapping in an IMC (action, key, triggers, modifiers, player-mappable) — inspection-first paired read so the agent verifies state instead of guessing; the primary verify tool. — `(context_path) -> {mappings:[{index, action, key, triggers[], modifiers[], player_mappable}]}` |
| `input_action_inspect` | P0 | python_run-backed TS | net-new moat | Read an Input Action's value_type, consume/pause flags, asset-level default triggers/modifiers, and description — verify-before-mutate for IA edits. — `(action_path) -> {value_type, consume_input, trigger_when_paused, triggers[], modifiers[], description}` |
| `input_list` | P0 | python_run-backed TS (EditorAssetLibrary) | net-new moat | List Input Action and Input Mapping Context assets under a path, paginated — discovery without a project scan; default 50/page. — `(dir?, kind?:Literal[action\|context\|all], limit?, offset?) -> {items:[{path, kind, value_type?}], has_more, next_offset}` |
| `input_list_keys` | P0 | python_run-backed TS | net-new moat (no peer enumerates FKeys) | Enumerate valid FKey names live from EKeys, filterable by device/category — kills the #1 agent failure of binding to an invalid key string like 'Space' vs 'SpaceBar'. — `(filter?, device?:Literal[keyboard\|mouse\|gamepad\|touch\|all], limit?, offset?) -> {keys:[{name, display_name, axis_type}], has_more}` |
| `input_validate` | P0 | python_run-backed TS + PLUMB validator wiring | net-new moat (beyond Flop auto-repair rules) | PLUMB-style conflict checker: duplicate key bindings, unbound actions, key bound to mismatched value-type, missing triggers, cross-context collisions — closed-loop correctness, the worldbuilding-grade moat. — `(context_path? \| dir?) -> {issues:[{code, severity, context, detail, fix_hint}]}` |
| `input_list_modifiers` | P1 | python_run-backed TS (reflection) | net-new moat (Houdini graph-intelligence analog) | Enumerate available UInputModifier subclasses with their editable UPROPERTYs and defaults — docs-as-a-tool so the agent knows DeadZone.LowerThreshold etc. before configuring; must run before *_set_props. — `() -> {modifiers:[{class, display, props:[{name, type, default}]}]}` |
| `input_list_triggers` | P1 | python_run-backed TS (reflection) | net-new moat | Enumerate available UInputTrigger subclasses (Pressed/Released/Hold/Tap/Chorded/Pulse) with editable props — reflection-driven discovery for trigger configuration. — `() -> {triggers:[{class, display, props:[{name, type, default}]}]}` |
| `input_unmap_key` | P1 | python_run-backed TS (rewrites mappings TArray) | net-new moat | Remove a key binding from an IMC by (action,key) — stable deletion that re-resolves indices; transaction-wrapped. — `(context_path, action_path, key) -> {removed:bool, remaining_count}` |
| `input_remap_key` | P1 | python_run-backed TS (array rewrite) | net-new moat | Rebind an existing mapping from old_key to new_key while preserving its triggers/modifiers — the rebinding primitive, the most-requested input edit. — `(context_path, action_path, old_key, new_key) -> {ok, mapping_index, new_key}` |
| `input_add_trigger` | P1 | C++ HaybaMCPToolkit/HaybaMCPInputHandler (net-new command) preferred; TS fallback rewrites array | net-new moat | Add a configured trigger (e.g. Hold with HoldTimeThreshold=0.5) to a specific mapping — must use C++ or whole-array rewrite due to the by-value map_key bug; this is where Python silently drops edits. — `(context_path, action_path, key, trigger_class, props?:object) -> {trigger, props}` |
| `input_add_modifier` | P1 | C++ HaybaMCPToolkit/HaybaMCPInputHandler (net-new) preferred; TS fallback | net-new moat | Add a configured modifier (DeadZone, Negate, SwizzleAxis, Scalar) to a mapping — the other half of axis authoring; same by-value caveat as triggers. — `(context_path, action_path, key, modifier_class, props?:object) -> {modifier, props}` |
| `input_remove_trigger` | P2 | python_run-backed TS (array rewrite) | net-new moat | Remove a trigger from a mapping by class or stack index — surgical cleanup of an over-configured binding. — `(context_path, action_path, key, trigger_class? , index?) -> {removed, remaining[]}` |
| `input_remove_modifier` | P2 | python_run-backed TS (array rewrite) | net-new moat | Remove a modifier from a mapping by class or stack index — surgical cleanup. — `(context_path, action_path, key, modifier_class?, index?) -> {removed, remaining[]}` |
| `input_set_trigger_props` | P2 | python_run-backed TS | net-new moat | Edit UPROPERTYs of an existing trigger instance on a mapping (e.g. tweak ActuationThreshold) without rebuilding the stack — fine-grained tuning. — `(context_path, action_path, key, index, props:object) -> {props}` |
| `input_set_modifier_props` | P2 | python_run-backed TS | net-new moat | Edit UPROPERTYs of an existing modifier instance on a mapping (e.g. DeadZone thresholds) in place — fine-grained tuning. — `(context_path, action_path, key, index, props:object) -> {props}` |
| `input_action_set_props` | P1 | python_run-backed TS | net-new moat | Set Input Action asset properties (value_type, consume_input, trigger_when_paused, reserve_all_mappings, description) with save — batch property authoring for IAs. — `(action_path, value_type?, consume_input?, trigger_when_paused?, reserve_all_mappings?, description?) -> {props}` |
| `input_action_set_default_triggers` | P2 | python_run-backed TS | net-new moat | Set the asset-level default triggers/modifiers carried by an Input Action itself (UE5.1+), applied to every mapping that uses it — reduces per-binding repetition. — `(action_path, triggers?:string[], modifiers?:string[]) -> {triggers[], modifiers[]}` |
| `input_batch_map` | P1 | python_run-backed TS (single save, transaction-wrapped) | matches Roblox mass_* batch verbs; net-new for Enhanced Input | Map many action->key rows (each with triggers/modifiers) into an IMC in one transaction with dry_run preview — outcome-verb that authors a whole scheme in a single call and one undo. — `(context_path, rows:[{action_path, key, triggers?, modifiers?}], dry_run?) -> {mapped, skipped, conflicts[], preview?}` |
| `input_apply_template` | P1 | python_run-backed TS | net-new moat (Maya parametric-prefab analog) | Scaffold a complete input scheme (fps_kbm, twin_stick_gamepad, topdown_kbm, platformer, vehicle) creating the IAs + IMC mappings + axis modifiers in one outcome call with dry_run — the 'create a working control scheme' headline verb. — `(dest_dir, template:Literal[fps_kbm\|twin_stick_gamepad\|topdown_kbm\|platformer\|vehicle], context_name?, dry_run?) -> {created_actions[], context_path, mappings[]}` |
| `input_clone_context` | P2 | python_run-backed TS | net-new moat | Duplicate an IMC to a new path, optionally remapping a whole device class (e.g. keyboard WASD -> gamepad stick) — fast keyboard+gamepad parity authoring. — `(src_context, dest_path, name, remap_device?:{from, to}) -> {path, remapped_count}` |
| `input_set_player_mappable` | P2 | python_run-backed TS (version-guarded) | net-new moat | Mark a binding as player-rebindable with a mappable name/display-name/category (UPlayerMappableKeySettings, 5.3+) — enables in-game rebind UIs, version-guarded. — `(context_path, action_path, key, mappable_name, display_name?, category?) -> {ok}` |
| `input_set_default_contexts` | P2 | python_run-backed TS (developer settings + save_config) | net-new moat | Register IMCs as project default mapping contexts with priorities via UEnhancedInputDeveloperSettings (5.3+) and persist to config — the design-time hook that makes contexts active at runtime without Blueprint wiring. — `(contexts:[{path, priority}], enabled?) -> {contexts[], config_path}` |
| `input_verify_binding` | P1 | python_run-backed TS | net-new moat | Assert a binding resolves exactly as expected (key + trigger set + modifier set) and return a structured diff — read-back verification primitive for agent self-correction and CI. — `(context_path, action_path, expect_key, expect_triggers?, expect_modifiers?) -> {match:bool, diff:{key?, triggers?, modifiers?}}` |
| `input_mapping_diff` | P2 | python_run-backed TS | net-new moat | Diff two IMCs (or before/after of one edit) returning added/removed/changed mappings — supports the vision-loop before/after pattern and review of agent edits. — `(context_a, context_b) -> {added[], removed[], changed[]}` |
| `input_transaction` | P1 | C++ HaybaMCPToolkit transaction seam (Theme-4C) | net-new moat vs Epic per-call undo | Bundle a sequence of input edits into ONE FScopedTransaction so the entire scheme reverts with a single Ctrl+Z — the named differentiator vs Epic per-call undo, applied to the input domain. — `(action:Literal[begin\|commit\|rollback], label?) -> {tx_id, state}` |
| `input_action_create_batch` | P2 | python_run-backed TS | matches Roblox mass_create_objects; net-new for Enhanced Input | Create many Input Actions in one call (name + value_type + description rows) in a single transaction — bulk IA scaffolding before mapping. — `(dir, actions:[{name, value_type, description?}], dry_run?) -> {created[], skipped[]}` |
| `input_rename` | P3 | python_run-backed TS | net-new moat | Rename/move an IA or IMC asset, fixing up references — unglamorous lifecycle housekeeping. — `(path, new_name, new_dir?) -> {new_path}` |
| `input_delete` | P3 | python_run-backed TS | net-new moat | Delete an IA or IMC with referencer check and dry_run, Plan-Mode gated — safe destructive cleanup that warns if a context still references the action. — `(path, dry_run?) -> {deleted, referencers[]}` |
| `input_export_scheme` | P3 | python_run-backed TS | net-new moat | Export an IMC (+ referenced IAs) to a portable JSON scheme for docs, review, or round-trip — enables versionable, diffable input design. — `(context_path, include_actions?) -> {scheme:object}` |
| `input_import_scheme` | P3 | python_run-backed TS | net-new moat | Author a full IMC + IAs from an exported/hand-written JSON scheme in one transaction with dry_run — declarative, idempotent input authoring (pairs with export). — `(dest_dir, scheme:object, dry_run?) -> {created_actions[], context_path, mappings[], conflicts[]}` |

**Dependencies:** EnhancedInput plugin enabled in the target project; Existing HaybaMCPInputHandler C++ commands (input_create_action/input_create_mapping/input_add_mapping) for the reference-semantics trigger/modifier path; Hardened python_run handler (SEH + 3-tier security gate + dangling-delegate gate) for the no-rebuild tools; Theme-1 add-a-tool refactor + pyTemplate factory so these land as single descriptors, not 7-file churn; Theme-4C transaction seam (FScopedTransaction begin/end) for single-undo bundling; PLUMB validator wiring for input_validate conflict rules; EditorAssetLibrary/AssetTools + save_asset for persistence (MarkPackageDirty + PostEditChange + save); UE 5.3+ for UPlayerMappableKeySettings and UEnhancedInputDeveloperSettings default-context array; Existing pie_input runtime injection tools to close the design->runtime test loop

**Risks:** map_key returns FEnhancedActionKeyMapping by value in Python (C++ returns a reference) -> modifiers/triggers added via the returned struct silently do NOT persist. Gate all trigger/modifier attach to C++ (HaybaMCPInputHandler) or a whole-array rewrite of the IMC 'mappings' TArray.; NewObject for UInputModifier/UInputTrigger must be outered to the owning IMC/IA or it GCs -> dangling-UObject crash class adjacent to #283/#284. Always pass the asset as outer and re-set the array property.; FKey names and validity vary by platform and installed plugins (gamepad, touch, XR). input_list_keys must enumerate live from EKeys at runtime, never ship a static key list.; Modifier/trigger UPROPERTY names differ per class (HoldTimeThreshold, ActuationThreshold, LowerThreshold, etc.). input_list_modifiers/triggers introspection must run BEFORE any *_set_props or the agent guesses wrong names.; PlayerMappableKeySettings API changed 5.1 (FName) -> 5.3 (UPlayerMappableKeySettings object). Version-guard input_set_player_mappable and degrade gracefully.; UEnhancedInputDeveloperSettings default mapping contexts only exist 5.3+ and persist to config; must call save_config / write DefaultInput.ini or the change is lost on restart.; Edits that skip MarkPackageDirty + save_asset are lost on editor restart; every write tool must dirty and the batch tools must save once at end.; Removing/reindexing mappings shifts mapping_index; tools keyed by index must re-resolve by (action,key) to stay stable across a batch.

**Live-editor test plan:** On a live editor, work in a scratch dir /Game/MCPTest/Input. 1) input_action_create IA_Jump(Boolean), IA_Move(Axis2D); input_action_inspect to confirm value_type round-trips. 2) input_context_create IMC_Default. 3) input_map_key IA_Jump=SpaceBar; input_map_key IA_Move for W/S/A/D with Negate + SwizzleAxis modifiers and per-key axis config. 4) input_context_inspect -> confirm 5 mappings with keys, triggers, modifiers persisted; open the IMC asset in the editor UI to visually verify the modifier stack actually saved (this is where the by-value Python bug shows). 5) input_validate -> expect zero issues, then deliberately double-bind SpaceBar and re-run to confirm the duplicate-key conflict is reported. 6) input_remap_key SpaceBar->Gamepad_FaceButton_Bottom and re-inspect. 7) input_apply_template fps_kbm with dry_run=true (preview only), then commit, then open assets. 8) Wrap a input_batch_map in input_transaction, then press Ctrl+Z once and confirm the entire batch reverts in a single undo. 9) Save all, restart editor, input_context_inspect again to prove persistence. 10) Close the loop: add IMC_Default via input_set_default_contexts (or BP), enter PIE, fire IA_Jump via the existing pie_input runtime injection, and confirm the action triggers — proving design-time authoring reaches runtime.


<a id="domain-22"></a>
## 22. AI / Navigation — Behavior Trees, Blackboards, NavMesh build/bounds, EQS, NavLink, AIController/Pawn perception

*34 tools — P0:8 P1:16 P2:9 P3:1*

This domain has a buried C++ asset (HaybaMCPBehaviorTreeHandler: bt_get_info/add_node/connect/compile) with no first-class wrapper, and zero coverage for Blackboards, NavMesh, EQS, NavLink, and perception — a complete competitive gap (only GenOrca touches BT/EQS at all; nobody documents NavMesh/perception). Strategy: (1) immediately surface the 4 stranded C++ BT commands as thin executeCommand wrappers (no rebuild, Theme 2 debt) with inspection-first read tools; (2) build the rest as python_run-backed pyTemplate descriptors over NavigationSystemV1, AIBlueprintHelperLibrary, AssetTools, and EditorActorSubsystem — no plugin rebuild; (3) lead with outcome-verbs (bt_build_from_spec → ONE undo, nav_setup → bounds+build+verify) and readback/verify tools (navmesh_project_point, navmesh_find_path) that prove the AI actually navigates, which is the credibility moat here. Every write tool ships a paired *_inspect, runs through PLUMB/validator, and wraps a transaction so multi-step BT authoring collapses into a single Ctrl+Z — directly beating Epic's per-call undo. Perception and EQS run-tools close the loop by executing in PIE and returning scored/perceived results as structured data plus optional vision capture.

| Tool | Pri | Module | Parity | Description / signature |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `bt_inspect` | P0 | thin TS over executeCommand('bt_get_info') — HaybaMCPToolkit/HaybaMCPBehaviorTreeHandler (no rebuild) | GenOrca (BT depth); net-new as a first-class wrapper (C++ command was stranded) | Read a Behavior Tree's full node tree, blackboard binding, and per-node GUID/class/children — the inspection-first read every BT edit verifies against. — `(path) -> {root_class, blackboard_path, nodes[{guid,name,class,parent_guid,children[]}], blackboard_keys[]}` |
| `bt_add_node` | P0 | thin TS over executeCommand('bt_add_node') — HaybaMCPToolkit/HaybaMCPBehaviorTreeHandler | GenOrca; net-new first-class wrapper | Add one composite/task/decorator/service node to a BT under a parent GUID, with class+kind sanity checks — surfaces the buried C++ authoring command. — `(path, node_kind, node_class_name, parent_guid?) -> {guid, class}` |
| `bt_connect` | P1 | thin TS over executeCommand('bt_connect') — HaybaMCPToolkit/HaybaMCPBehaviorTreeHandler | GenOrca; net-new first-class wrapper | Attach an existing BT node as a child of a composite, with built-in cycle rejection — prevents the stack-overflow crash class on later walks. — `(path, parent_guid, child_guid) -> {ok}` |
| `bt_validate` | P1 | thin TS over executeCommand('bt_compile') — HaybaMCPToolkit/HaybaMCPBehaviorTreeHandler | Flop (bp_dry_run analog); net-new for BT | Validate/compile a BT (root present, blackboard bound, kismet errors if backed by BP) and return errors/warnings — dry-run gate before relying on the tree. — `(path) -> {ok, errors[], warnings[]}` |
| `bt_create` | P0 | python_run-backed TS descriptor (AssetToolsHelpers + BehaviorTreeFactory) | net-new moat (no competitor creates BT assets) | Create a new BehaviorTree asset, optionally creating+binding a BlackboardData in the same call — the entry point for AI authoring. — `(package_path, name, blackboard_path?, create_blackboard?) -> {bt_path, blackboard_path}` |
| `bt_build_from_spec` | P1 | python_run-backed TS descriptor wrapping bt_add_node/bt_connect under FScopedTransaction (HaybaMCPToolkit transaction seam) | net-new moat (single-undo bundling beats Epic per-call undo) | Author an entire BT (composites/tasks/decorators/services + blackboard bindings) from one JSON spec inside a single transaction → ONE Ctrl+Z. Outcome-verb over the low-level add/connect calls. — `(path, spec{root,children[...]}, dry_run?) -> {created_guids[], warnings[], undo_token}` |
| `bt_set_node_property` | P1 | python_run-backed TS descriptor (resolve node by deterministic GUID, set_editor_property) | net-new moat | Set properties on a BT node by GUID (e.g., MoveTo acceptable radius, Wait time, blackboard key selectors) — the missing 'configure' half of node authoring. — `(path, guid, properties{}) -> {ok, applied{}, skipped{}}` |
| `bt_set_decorator` | P1 | python_run-backed TS descriptor | net-new moat | Configure a Blackboard-based decorator (observed key, key query, operation, notify-observer mode) on a node — the most common BT condition wiring. — `(path, decorator_guid, blackboard_key, operation, key_query?) -> {ok}` |
| `bt_remove_node` | P2 | python_run-backed TS descriptor (transactional) | net-new moat | Remove a BT node (and its subtree/decorators/services) by GUID inside a transaction — the destructive counterpart, Plan-Mode gated. — `(path, guid, dry_run?) -> {removed_guids[], ok}` |
| `bt_list_node_classes` | P1 | python_run-backed TS descriptor (iterate UClass subclasses, paginated) | net-new moat (fixes postmortem 'no reflection-introspection' gap) | List available BTTask/BTDecorator/BTService/BTComposite subclasses (engine + project) for use as node_class_name — introspection so the agent stops guessing class paths. — `(kind?, name_filter?, limit=50, offset?) -> {classes[{name,path,kind}], has_more, next_offset}` |
| `blackboard_create` | P0 | python_run-backed TS descriptor (BlackboardDataFactory) | net-new moat | Create a BlackboardData asset, optionally inheriting a parent blackboard — required substrate for any BT. — `(package_path, name, parent_path?) -> {blackboard_path}` |
| `blackboard_add_key` | P0 | python_run-backed TS descriptor (FBlackboardEntry + BlackboardKeyType_*) | net-new moat | Add a typed key (Bool/Int/Float/Vector/Rotator/Name/String/Object/Class/Enum) with optional instance-sync — the data the whole BT reads/writes. — `(blackboard_path, key_name, key_type, base_class?, instance_synced?) -> {ok, keys[]}` |
| `blackboard_remove_key` | P2 | python_run-backed TS descriptor (transactional) | net-new moat | Remove a blackboard key by name inside a transaction, warning if BT decorators reference it — destructive, Plan-Mode gated. — `(blackboard_path, key_name, dry_run?) -> {ok, removed, referencing_nodes[]}` |
| `blackboard_inspect` | P1 | python_run-backed TS descriptor | net-new moat | List all keys (name/type/base-class/instance-synced) of a BlackboardData — paired read for every blackboard mutation. — `(blackboard_path) -> {parent_path, keys[{name,type,base_class,instance_synced}]}` |
| `navmesh_add_bounds_volume` | P0 | python_run-backed TS descriptor (EditorActorSubsystem.spawn_actor_from_class NavMeshBoundsVolume + brush extent) | net-new moat | Spawn/resize a NavMeshBoundsVolume to cover given bounds or the whole level — without this NavMesh silently builds nothing (the AI equivalent of the PCG-needs-Landscape footgun). — `(origin?, extent?, cover_level?) -> {actor_path, bounds}` |
| `navmesh_build` | P0 | python_run-backed TS descriptor over the Theme-0 job-envelope (NavigationSystemV1.build / Build Paths) | remiphilippe (headless build loop); net-new for nav | Rebuild navigation data (dirty or full) via the async job-envelope, returning a job id — never blocks the game thread like the legacy build freezes. — `(mode='dirty'\|'full', wait?) -> {job_id, status} \| (on wait) {ok, tiles_built, duration_ms}` |
| `navmesh_inspect` | P1 | python_run-backed TS descriptor (NavigationSystemV1 + RecastNavMesh introspection) | net-new moat | Report nav coverage: bounds volumes, RecastNavMesh tiles built, navigable area, agent config, and whether a probe point is reachable — proof the navmesh exists and is usable. — `(probe_point?) -> {bounds_volumes[], tiles_built, navigable_area, agent{radius,height,cell_size}, probe_on_navmesh?}` |
| `navmesh_set_config` | P1 | python_run-backed TS descriptor (set_editor_property on RecastNavMesh) | net-new moat | Set RecastNavMesh agent radius/height/step/cell-size/tile-size and runtime-generation mode, then mark dirty — tunes nav to the pawn before rebuild. — `(agent_radius?, agent_height?, agent_max_step?, cell_size?, tile_size?, runtime_generation?) -> {ok, applied{}}` |
| `navmesh_project_point` | P1 | python_run-backed TS descriptor (NavigationSystemV1.project_point_to_navigation) | net-new moat | Project a world point onto the navmesh within a query extent and return the nearest navigable point — core readback/verify for placement and pathing. — `(point, query_extent?) -> {on_navmesh, projected_point, distance}` |
| `navmesh_find_path` | P1 | python_run-backed TS descriptor (NavigationSystemV1.find_path_to_location_synchronously / GetPathLength) | net-new moat | Test pathfinding between two points and return length, point list, and partial/no-path flags — verifies the navmesh is actually traversable, not just present. — `(start, end, agent_radius?) -> {found, is_partial, length, points[]}` |
| `navmesh_random_reachable_point` | P2 | python_run-backed TS descriptor (get_random_reachable_point_in_radius) | Roblox character_navigation (loose analog); net-new for UE | Sample a random navigable point reachable within a radius of an origin — spawn-point selection and reachability sanity checks. — `(origin, radius, count=1) -> {points[], found}` |
| `nav_setup` | P1 | python_run-backed TS descriptor composing add_bounds_volume + build + inspect (transactional) | net-new moat (outcome-over-operations) | One outcome-verb: add a bounds volume over the level, build navigation, and verify coverage>0 — fails loudly if the level produces no navmesh. — `(extent?, agent_radius?, dry_run?) -> {bounds_volume, tiles_built, navigable_area, ok}` |
| `ai_spawn_controller` | P0 | python_run-backed TS descriptor (EditorActorSubsystem spawn + AIController.possess / AIBlueprintHelperLibrary) | net-new moat | Spawn an AIController and possess a target pawn, optionally running a BT immediately — the bridge that makes a pawn act on AI. — `(pawn_actor, controller_class?, behavior_tree?) -> {controller_path, possessed, bt_running}` |
| `ai_run_behavior_tree` | P1 | python_run-backed TS descriptor (AIBlueprintHelperLibrary.run_behavior_tree) | net-new moat | Assign and start a BT on an AIController or pawn (PIE/sim), creating the blackboard component — exercises the authored tree end-to-end. — `(target_actor, behavior_tree, requires_pie=true) -> {ok, blackboard_keys[]}` |
| `ai_set_blackboard_value` | P2 | python_run-backed TS descriptor (BlackboardComponent.set_value_as_* in PIE world) | net-new moat | Set a runtime blackboard value (bool/int/float/vector/object/name) on a running AI during PIE — the primary AI-debug lever to drive/observe behavior. — `(target_actor, key_name, value, requires_pie=true) -> {ok, previous}` |
| `ai_inspect` | P1 | python_run-backed TS descriptor (AIController + BrainComponent + BlackboardComponent introspection) | net-new moat | Read AI runtime state for an actor: possessing controller, active BT + current/running task, and all blackboard values — inspection-first for AI debugging. — `(target_actor) -> {controller, behavior_tree, active_task, blackboard{}}` |
| `ai_perception_configure` | P1 | python_run-backed TS descriptor (AIPerceptionComponent + AISenseConfig_Sight/Hearing on the asset CDO/BP) | net-new moat | Add/configure an AIPerceptionComponent on a pawn/controller class with sight/hearing/damage sense configs (radius, angle, affiliation) — wires what the AI can detect. — `(target_class_or_actor, senses[{type,radius,angle,affiliation}], dominant_sense?) -> {ok, configured_senses[]}` |
| `ai_perception_query` | P2 | python_run-backed TS descriptor (AIPerceptionComponent.get_currently_perceived_actors / get_actors_perception) | net-new moat | Return actors currently perceived by an AI (by sense, with last-stimulus location/age) during PIE — closes the perception loop with verifiable results. — `(target_actor, sense?, requires_pie=true) -> {perceived[{actor,sense,location,age,successfully_sensed}]}` |
| `navlink_add` | P2 | python_run-backed TS descriptor (spawn NavLinkProxy + PointLinks config) | net-new moat | Spawn and configure a NavLinkProxy (simple or smart link) with start/end points, direction, and area class — connects disjoint navmesh regions (jumps/ledges). — `(start, end, direction='both'\|'left'\|'right', area_class?) -> {actor_path, link_id}` |
| `navlink_inspect` | P3 | python_run-backed TS descriptor | net-new moat | List NavLinkProxy actors in the level with their endpoints/direction/area class — paired read + verify links are placed where intended. — `(limit=50, offset?) -> {links[{actor,start,end,direction,area_class}], has_more, next_offset}` |
| `eqs_create` | P2 | python_run-backed TS descriptor (EnvQueryFactory + preset instantiation) | GenOrca (EQS mention); net-new authoring (engine-limit flagged) | Create an EnvQuery asset from a built-in template/preset (generator + test set), parameterized — works within UE Python's EQS-graph-authoring ceiling (template-scoped, flagged). — `(package_path, name, template, params?) -> {eqs_path, generator, tests[]}` |
| `eqs_run` | P2 | python_run-backed TS descriptor (EnvQueryManager.run_query, PIE/sim) | net-new moat | Run an EQS query at a querier/context location and return ranked items with scores — proves the query produces sensible results before BT integration. — `(eqs_path, querier_actor?, context_location?, run_mode='all_matching') -> {items[{location,score}], best}` |
| `ai_batch_assign_bt` | P2 | python_run-backed TS descriptor (EditorActorSubsystem query loop, transactional) | Roblox mass_set_property (batch analog); net-new for UE AI | Assign one BT (and optional blackboard) to many AIControllers/pawns selected by class/tag/query in a single transaction — batch op for populating a level with behavior. — `(behavior_tree, select_by{class?,tag?,actors?}, dry_run?) -> {assigned[], skipped[], count}` |
| `ai_nav_validate` | P1 | python_run-backed TS descriptor feeding the PLUMB/validator pipeline | net-new moat (PLUMB quantified validation extended to AI) | Domain validator: cross-checks BT/blackboard/nav/perception consistency (missing blackboard binding, decorator keys not in blackboard, no nav coverage, BT class refs unresolved) and returns an unsat-core style report — PLUMB-style validate-before-commit for AI. — `(scope{bt?,blackboard?,level?}) -> {ok, issues[{severity,code,where,fix}]}` |

**Dependencies:** NavigationSystem + AIModule plugins enabled in the project (RecastNavMesh, NavMeshBoundsVolume, AIController); python_run hardened handler (SEH + security gate) — vehicle for ~26 of these tools with no rebuild; Existing C++ HaybaMCPBehaviorTreeHandler (bt_get_info/add_node/connect/compile) for the 4 thin wrappers + future bt_remove/set-property C++ additions; Add-a-tool refactor (Theme 1): ToolDescriptor with pyTemplate factory + auto-collected descriptors[] barrel + generated sidecar; executeCommand/tool-executor.ts (ue_command + ue_legacy dispatch) and Code-Mode/deferred routing pack registration so these land behind hayba_invoke, not the eager surface; editor_start_pie + editor_capture_viewport/render_camera for PIE-time perception/EQS/path validation and vision feedback; PLUMB validator + transaction (FScopedTransaction) seam for begin/commit/undo bundling; unreal Python: NavigationSystemV1, AIBlueprintHelperLibrary, EnvQueryManager, AssetToolsHelpers, EditorActorSubsystem, EditorAssetLibrary

**Risks:** UE Python cannot author EQS generator/test graphs or BTService logic from scratch — like Niagara/ControlRig this is an engine limit; scope EQS/BT-service tools to template-instantiation + parameter packs and flag as engine limitation honestly (no second not_implemented_in_v1).; NavMesh build can be long-running and game-thread-bound — must go through the Theme-0 job-envelope/async pattern, not a blocking 300s call, or it freezes the editor like build_*/test_run.; Perception/EQS/path queries are only meaningful at runtime — many require PIE; editor-time calls return stale or empty results. Tools must declare requires_pie and gate accordingly.; BT node identity uses deterministic GUIDs derived from path (GuidForNode); renaming/reparenting changes the path and invalidates cached GUIDs across calls — readback after every mutation, never cache GUIDs across structural edits.; NavMeshBoundsVolume sizing wrong (too small/large) silently yields no navigable area; nav_setup must verify coverage>0 and return actionable error, mirroring the PCG SurfaceSampler-needs-Landscape footgun.; NavLinkProxy and AIPerceptionComponent often need a Blueprint/Pawn class rather than a placed actor instance to persist; component-add on a transient PIE actor won't save — target the asset/CDO where persistence is required.; Decorator-on-task attachment is constrained in the C++ handler (requires composite parent); pyTemplate authoring must mirror those rules or produce confusing failures.

**Live-editor test plan:** On a live editor with a navigable test level: (1) blackboard_create + blackboard_add_key (Bool 'HasTarget', Object 'TargetActor', Vector 'MoveTo'); blackboard_inspect confirms keys/types. (2) bt_create bound to that blackboard; bt_build_from_spec to author Selector>Sequence>MoveTo+Wait with a Blackboard decorator in one call; bt_inspect (wraps bt_get_info) confirms node tree + parent GUIDs; bt_validate (wraps bt_compile) returns ok=true, errors=[]. Undo once — entire BT structure reverts (single-Ctrl+Z proof). (3) nav_setup over the level bounds → navmesh_inspect shows tiles>0 and area>0; navmesh_project_point on a floor point returns a near point with on_navmesh=true; navmesh_find_path between two floor points returns is_partial=false and length>0; intentionally pick an unreachable point and confirm is_partial/no_path. (4) ai_spawn_controller possessing a test pawn + ai_run_behavior_tree with the authored BT; start PIE via editor_start_pie; ai_set_blackboard_value sets HasTarget=true and the pawn moves; editor_capture_viewport screenshots motion. (5) ai_perception_configure adds a sight sense to the pawn; in PIE ai_perception_query returns the seen target actor. (6) eqs_create from a template + eqs_run at a context location returns scored items sorted by score. Cross-check every read tool's JSON against the in-editor BT/Blackboard/Nav debug views; run validator_run before/after each mutating call.


<a id="domain-23"></a>
## 23. Gameplay Ability System

*37 tools — P0:13 P1:12 P2:11 P3:1*

GAS is "zero coverage" as first-class tools, yet a real C++ plugin already exists (unreal/HaybaMCPGAS/.../HaybaMCPGASHandler.cpp) shipping exactly 4 raw commands (gas_create_ability/grant_ability/create_effect/apply_effect) reachable only via hayba_invoke ue_legacy — so step one is honest first-class wrappers plus inspection/read-back over that surface, step two broadens to the full author loop (AttributeSets, gameplay tags, GameplayCues, ability-set data assets, runtime queries). No competitor (Aura, Flop, GenOrca, UAIP, Epic 5.8) ships GAS authoring at all, so the entire domain is net-new moat; we differentiate with PLUMB validation, transaction-wrapping, and inspection-first ergonomics. Strategy: keep the crash-prone runtime/CDO-mutation ops in C++ (extend the existing handler under the ProcessCommand SEH seam), and push everything authoring/introspection that UE Python's GameplayAbilities reflection plus EditorAssetSubsystem can reach into python_run-backed TS wrappers (no rebuild). Every write tool ships a paired *_inspect read tool and a dry_run flag; tag operations route through the native GameplayTagsManager so we never desync the project tag table.

| Tool | Pri | Module | Parity | Description / signature |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `gas_inspect` | P0 | python_run-backed TS (EditorUtility + reflection); falls back to gas_describe in HaybaMCPGAS | net-new moat | Probe the GAS subsystem: is GameplayAbilities enabled, which AttributeSet/ASC C++ classes exist, count of GA/GE assets and registered gameplay tags. Gating probe so the agent knows the domain is usable before any write. — `() -> {plugin_enabled, asc_classes[], attributeset_classes[], ability_count, effect_count, tag_count}` |
| `gas_ability_create` | P0 | HaybaMCPGAS (existing GasCreateAbility, add policy/tag params + FScopedTransaction) | net-new moat (extends existing gas_create_ability) | Create a UGameplayAbility blueprint at a content path with optional instancing/net-execution policy and cost/cooldown effect refs. First-class wrapper over the existing gas_create_ability C++ command (today only via ue_legacy). — `(path, name, instancing_policy?, net_execution_policy?, dry_run?) -> {path, name, ok}` |
| `gas_ability_inspect` | P0 | python_run-backed TS (load BP GeneratedClass CDO, reflect tag containers) | net-new moat | Read back a GameplayAbility blueprint CDO: instancing policy, ability/cancel/block/activation tags, cost/cooldown GE, triggers. Paired read tool so edits are verifiable (inspection-first invariant). — `(ability_path) -> {instancing, tags:{ability,cancel,block,activation_required,activation_blocked}, cost_effect, cooldown_effect, triggers[]}` |
| `gas_ability_set_tags` | P0 | python_run-backed TS (CDO FGameplayTagContainer set + MarkPackageDirty + recompile) | net-new moat | Set the AbilityTags / CancelAbilitiesWithTag / BlockAbilitiesWithTag / ActivationOwned/Required/Blocked tag containers on an ability CDO. Honest workaround for the hard limit that Python cannot create AttributeSet classes — flagged as requiring a recompile. — `(module_name, class_name, attributes:[{name, default}], dest_dir?) -> {header_path, source_path, needs_compile:true}` |
| `gas_tag_list` | P0 | python_run-backed TS (GameplayTagsManager.request_all_gameplay_tags) | net-new moat | List registered gameplay tags from GameplayTagsManager, optionally filtered by a parent prefix, paginated. | `gas_tag_add` | P0 | python_run-backed TS (GameplayTagsManager add_new_gameplay_tag_to_ini) | net-new moat | Add a gameplay tag (and implicitly its parents) to the project tag table (Config/DefaultGameplayTags.ini) with an optional dev comment; validates the tag string and dry-runs. The authoring half of tag-driven grants. — `(tag, comment?, source_ini?, dry_run?) -> {tag, added, parents_created[]}` |
| `gas_tag_remove` | P2 | python_run-backed TS (GameplayTagsManager remove + ref scan) | net-new moat | Remove a gameplay tag from the project ini tag table after checking for references. | `gas_tag_validate` | P1 | python_run-backed TS (GameplayTagsManager request_gameplay_tag is_valid) | net-new moat | Check whether one or more tag strings are well-formed and registered, returning unknown/typo candidates — prevents silently binding a misspelled tag to an ability. — `(tags[]) -> {results:[{tag, valid, registered, suggestions[]}]}` |

The primary debug/verify tool for the runtime side. — `(actor_path) -> {has_asc, abilities[], active_effects[], owned_tags[], attributes[]}` |
| `gas_asc_add` | P2 | HaybaMCPGAS (new AddComponentByClass + InitAbilityActorInfo) | net-new moat | Add a UAbilitySystemComponent to an editor actor (or its blueprint) if it lacks one, so subsequent grant/apply tools have a target. High-leverage outcome-verb for GAS-heavy projects. — `(path, name, abilities[], effects[], attribute_sets[], dry_run?) -> {path, entry_counts}` |
| `gas_batch_grant` | P2 | HaybaMCPGAS (new iterate matched actors) over the grant/apply core | GenOrca/Roblox have batch ops generally; GAS batch is net-new moat | Grant a set of abilities (or apply a set of effects) across many actors matched by class/tag/selection in one call, with per-actor results. Batch outcome-verb (matches Roblox mass_* peers). — `(actor_query, ability_paths?[], effect_paths?[], level?, dry_run?) -> {results:[{actor, granted, applied, error?}], total}` |
| `gas_list_assets` | P1 | python_run-backed TS (AssetRegistry filter by class) | net-new moat | List GameplayAbility / GameplayEffect / GameplayCue / AbilitySet assets in the project with filter + pagination, classifying each. Catalog/discovery tool so the agent can find what to edit. — `(kind?, path_filter?, limit?, offset?) -> {assets[], has_more, next_offset}` |
| `gas_validate` | P1 | python_run-backed TS feeding validator_run + PLUMB constraints | net-new moat (PLUMB/validator integration none match) | PLUMB-style quantified check of a GAS asset or actor: ability references a registered cost/cooldown GE, all bound tags are registered, effect modifiers reference real attributes, no dangling class refs. Closed-loop validate-before/after-mutate moat. — `(target_path?, actor_path?, fix?) -> {ok, violations:[{code, message, severity}], fixed[]}` |

**Dependencies:** GameplayAbilities plugin enabled in the target project (uplugin dependency); HaybaMCPGAS plugin module already present and compiled (HaybaMCPGASHandler.cpp ships gas_create_ability/grant_ability/create_effect/apply_effect); AttributeSet subclasses are C++-defined in the target game (UE Python cannot author new UAttributeSet C++ classes) — attribute-creation tools scope to inspection plus a codegen-stub emitter; GameplayTagsManager + Config/DefaultGameplayTags.ini for tag CRUD; EditorAssetSubsystem / AssetRegistry for asset discovery and CDO default reads; FScopedTransaction undo seam (brief Theme 4C) for wrapping C++ mutations; PLUMB validator + validator_run + hayba_invoke ue_legacy fallthrough; Code-Mode / deferred routing + a new 'gas' pack so the surface stays off the eager context; ProcessCommand SEH wrap (Theme 0) so all GAS C++ handlers are crash-recoverable; Theme 1 add-a-tool descriptor refactor (pyTemplate factory) to land the python wrappers without 7-file churn

**Risks:** GameplayAbilities runtime types (UAbilitySystemComponent, GiveAbility, ApplyGameplayEffectToSelf) are largely NOT exposed to UE Python — most runtime grant/apply/query ops must stay C++ in the existing handler; python wrappers there are passthroughs over ue_legacy, not real python; GAS ASCs normally only exist on PIE/runtime actors; editor-world actors rarely have a live ASC, so grant/apply/query tools must clearly state they target the PIE world or an editor actor that explicitly hosts an ASC, and fail with an actionable message otherwise; New UAttributeSet classes cannot be created from Python (need C++ + recompile) — attribute_create can only emit a C++ stub + edit config; over-promising here would be a second not_implemented_in_v1 honesty violation; Setting CDO defaults on GameplayEffect/Ability blueprints can be lost without MarkPackageDirty + save and a blueprint recompile; read-back verify is mandatory or edits silently no-op; Gameplay tag edits write Config/DefaultGameplayTags.ini and require GameplayTagsManager reload; concurrent/native tags can collide and a malformed tag can corrupt the project tag table — must validate tag strings and dry_run; SetByCaller / ScalableFloat / ModMagnitudeCalculation magnitude types are intricate; a naive float-only modifier API will mislead users into thinking complex magnitudes are supported; ApplyGameplayEffectToSelf with a long-lived ASC during editor (non-PIE) can leak ActiveGameplayEffect handles; apply tools must be PIE-gated or transaction-scoped; Blueprint-graph authoring of ability logic (ActivateAbility event graph) hits the same K2-graph limitation as the broader product — scope ability tools to class creation + CDO/property config + tag wiring, not node-graph logic

**Live-editor test plan:** On a live editor with the GameplayAbilities plugin and a project that has at least one C++ UAttributeSet (e.g. the Lyra/ASC sample or a scratch GAS pawn): (1) gas_inspect to confirm the plugin + ASC classes resolve; (2) gas_ability_create /Game/GAS/GA_Test then gas_ability_inspect to read back the new blueprint CDO (tags, instancing policy) — assert path exists in AssetRegistry; (3) gas_attributeset_list + gas_attribute_list to enumerate the project's C++ attributes, pick Health; (4) gas_effect_create a Duration effect with an Additive Health modifier via gas_effect_add_modifier, then gas_effect_inspect to verify DurationPolicy + modifier read-back; (5) gas_tag_add 'Ability.Test.Fire', gas_tag_list to confirm it landed in DefaultGameplayTags.ini, gas_ability_set_tags to bind it as the ability's AssetTag; (6) enter PIE (editor_start_pie), find the player ASC actor, gas_ability_grant the ability and gas_effect_apply the effect, then gas_asc_inspect / gas_active_effects_list / gas_attribute_get to confirm Health changed and the granted ability spec + active GE handle appear; (7) gas_ability_try_activate by tag and confirm via gas_asc_inspect; (8) run validator_run before/after each mutate and confirm transaction wrapping by issuing an undo; (9) re-run every *_inspect with dry_run on the create tools to confirm no asset is written. Capture editor viewport / PIE screenshot where attribute changes are visible (e.g. a debug HUD) to close the vision loop.


<a id="domain-24"></a>
## 24. Gameplay Framework defaults — GameMode/GameState/PlayerController/Pawn/Character/HUD/GameInstance/PlayerStart + World Settings default-class wiring

*29 tools — P0:7 P1:9 P2:10 P3:3*

This domain is near-greenfield: today there is NO way to set the GameMode, configure World Settings, or wire any default class (Pawn/Controller/HUD/GameState/GameInstance) — level_info only reads. That is gating, because nothing an agent builds is playable until the framework is wired. Strategy: ship the inspection-first read pair plus the three write surfaces UE actually uses (project GameMapsSettings ini, per-level WorldSettings override, and GameMode CDO class-wiring), all python_run-backed so there is zero plugin rebuild. The moat is gameframework_verify (dry-run resolution of which classes the engine will actually pick through the world-override > project-default > engine chain) and PLUMB-style gameframework_validate (abstract pawn? no PlayerStart? unassigned controller?), which no competitor offers for the gameplay framework. Cap it with one-shot outcome verbs (scaffold, apply_preset) and a checkpoint/restore pair, since project-ini edits sit outside UE's Ctrl+Z undo and need an explicit snapshot for safety.

| Tool | Pri | Module | Parity | Description / signature |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `gameframework_inspect` | P0 | python_run-backed TS: src/tools/gameframework/inspect.ts | net-new moat (level_info only reads; no competitor exposes framework resolution) | Read the full effective framework wiring in one call — project GameMapsSettings (GlobalDefaultGameMode, GameInstanceClass, default maps), the active level's WorldSettings override, and the resolved effective Pawn/Controller/HUD/GameState/PlayerState classes; the inspection-first read for the whole domain. — `(level_path?, include_resolution=true) -> {project:{global_default_gamemode,game_instance_class,editor_startup_map,game_default_map,transition_map}, world_settings:{default_gamemode_override,kill_z,gravity_z}, effective:{gamemode,pawn,controller,hud,gamestate,playerstate,spectator}, playerstart_count}` |
| `gameframework_verify` | P0 | python_run-backed TS: src/tools/gameframework/verify.ts | net-new moat | Dry-run resolver: answer 'if I hit PIE on this level right now, exactly which GameMode/Pawn/Controller/HUD/GameState will the engine instantiate' by reproducing the world-override > project-default > engine-fallback precedence; the read-back/verify safety tool. — `(level_path?) -> {effective_gamemode, resolved_from:'world_override'\|'project_default'\|'engine', pawn, controller, hud, gamestate, playerstate, warnings[]}` |
| `gameframework_validate` | P1 | python_run-backed TS wrapping validator_run/plumb_validate: src/tools/gameframework/validate.ts | net-new moat (PLUMB validate > Flop auto-repair) | Run PLUMB/validator-style checks over the framework wiring — abstract/deprecated default classes, missing PlayerStart, unassigned PlayerControllerClass, GameMode not referenced by any default, pawn auto-possess conflicts; returns actionable issues with codes. — `(level_path?, fix=false) -> {ok, issues:[{code,severity,message,subject}], checked}` |
| `project_set_default_gamemode` | P0 | python_run-backed TS (GameMapsSettings CDO + save_config): src/tools/gameframework/project-set-default-gamemode.ts | Flop/GenOrca have project-settings writes; this specific wiring is net-new for Hayba | Set GlobalDefaultGameMode in GameMapsSettings (DefaultEngine.ini) — the headline missing capability; this is how you set the GameMode project-wide. — `(gamemode_class, plan_approved=false) -> {ok, previous, ini_path, needs_restart:false, diff}` |
| `project_gamemaps_get` | P1 | python_run-backed TS: src/tools/gameframework/project-gamemaps-get.ts | parity with project-settings readers | Read-back of GameMapsSettings (GlobalDefaultGameMode, GameInstanceClass, EditorStartupMap, GameDefaultMap, ServerDefaultMap, TransitionMap); paired reader for the project-level setters. — `() -> {global_default_gamemode, game_instance_class, editor_startup_map, game_default_map, server_default_map, transition_map}` |
| `project_set_game_instance` | P1 | python_run-backed TS: src/tools/gameframework/project-set-game-instance.ts | net-new | Set the GameInstanceClass in GameMapsSettings — required to wire persistent/session-level subsystems; warns that this only fully applies after an editor restart. — `(game_instance_class, plan_approved=false) -> {ok, previous, needs_restart:true, diff}` |
| `project_set_default_maps` | P2 | python_run-backed TS: src/tools/gameframework/project-set-default-maps.ts | net-new | Set EditorStartupMap / GameDefaultMap / ServerDefaultMap / TransitionMap in one call; controls what loads on launch and PIE-from-empty. — `(editor_startup?, game_default?, server_default?, transition?, plan_approved=false) -> {ok, previous, diff}` |
| `world_settings_set_gamemode` | P0 | python_run-backed TS (world.get_world_settings + level_save): src/tools/gameframework/world-settings-set-gamemode.ts | net-new (no World Settings writer exists today) | Set DefaultGameMode override on the active (persistent) level's WorldSettings — the per-level way to configure the GameMode; the named 'configure World Settings' gap. — `(gamemode_class, level_path?, save=true) -> {ok, previous_override, level_saved}` |
| `world_settings_get` | P1 | python_run-backed TS: src/tools/gameframework/world-settings-get.ts | net-new | Read the persistent level's WorldSettings — default GameMode override, gravity override, kill-Z, world-to-meters, world bounds checks, default physics volume; gameframework-specific read that level_info doesn't cover. — `(level_path?) -> {default_gamemode_override, global_gravity_z, enable_world_bounds_checks, kill_z, world_to_meters, default_physics_volume_class}` |

| `framework_create_blueprint` | P1 | python_run-backed TS: src/tools/gameframework/framework-create-blueprint.ts | DCC peers (Maya parametric prefab); net-new for UE framework | Create a Blueprint subclass of any framework base (Character/Pawn/PlayerController/HUD/GameState/PlayerState/GameInstance/SpectatorPawn) — one outcome verb covering the whole class family instead of N near-identical creators. — `(asset_path, base:'Character'\|'Pawn'\|'PlayerController'\|'HUD'\|'GameState'\|'PlayerState'\|'GameInstance'\|'SpectatorPawn'\|'DefaultPawn', parent_override?) -> {ok, asset_path, generated_class}` |
| `framework_blueprint_list` | P2 | python_run-backed TS (AssetRegistry query): src/tools/gameframework/framework-blueprint-list.ts | net-new | List all Blueprints in the project derived from framework bases, grouped by kind (GameMode/Pawn/Controller/...); discovery for the wiring tools so agents don't guess class paths. — `(kind?, limit=50, offset=0) -> {items:[{asset_path, generated_class, kind, parent}], has_more, next_offset}` |
| `character_configure_defaults` | P1 | python_run-backed TS (CDO -> CharacterMovement subobject): src/tools/gameframework/character-configure-defaults.ts | net-new | Set CharacterMovementComponent defaults (max walk speed, jump Z velocity, gravity scale, air control, rotation rate, orient-to-movement) on a Character BP CDO; the common 'make the character feel right' verb. — `(character_path, max_walk_speed?, jump_z?, gravity_scale?, air_control?, rotation_rate?, orient_to_movement?) -> {ok, applied[], previous{}}` |
| `pawn_set_controller_rotation` | P2 | python_run-backed TS: src/tools/gameframework/pawn-set-controller-rotation.ts | net-new | Set bUseControllerRotationYaw/Pitch/Roll and AutoPossessPlayer/AutoPossessAI on a Pawn or Character CDO; controls camera-follows-controller behavior that breaks silently if wrong. — `(pawn_path, use_yaw?, use_pitch?, use_roll?, auto_possess_player?, auto_possess_ai?) -> {ok, applied[], previous{}}` |
| `playercontroller_configure` | P2 | python_run-backed TS: src/tools/gameframework/playercontroller-configure.ts | net-new | Set PlayerController CDO defaults — default Enhanced Input MappingContext (added in BP-construction note), show-mouse-cursor, default input mode, click/touch events enabled; wires the input front door. — `(controller_path, show_mouse_cursor?, enable_click_events?, enable_touch_events?, default_mapping_context?) -> {ok, applied[], previous{}}` |
| `framework_set_cdo_property` | P3 | python_run-backed TS: src/tools/gameframework/framework-set-cdo-property.ts | Roblox mass_set_property analog; net-new for UE CDOs | Validated escape-hatch: set any editor property on any framework BP's CDO by name, type-checked against reflection before write; covers the long tail without a new tool per property. — `(asset_path, property, value) -> {ok, previous, applied}` |
| `playerstart_spawn` | P0 | python_run-backed TS (actor_spawn PlayerStart): src/tools/gameframework/playerstart-spawn.ts | actor_spawn exists generically; framework-typed convenience is net-new | Place a PlayerStart actor at a transform with an optional PlayerStartTag; without one, PIE has nowhere to spawn the pawn — the most-forgotten required actor. — `(location?, rotation?, tag?, label?) -> {ok, actor_path, tag}` |
| `playerstart_list` | P1 | python_run-backed TS: src/tools/gameframework/playerstart-list.ts | net-new | List PlayerStart actors in the level with their tags and transforms; paired reader to confirm spawn points exist and are tagged for multiplayer. — `(level_path?) -> {items:[{actor_path, tag, location, rotation}], count}` |
| `playerstart_batch_spawn` | P2 | python_run-backed TS: src/tools/gameframework/playerstart-batch-spawn.ts | Roblox mass_create_objects analog | Place multiple PlayerStarts at once (grid/ring/list of transforms) with auto-incremented tags; sets up multiplayer spawn points in one call. — `(points:[{location,rotation?,tag?}], layout?:'list'\|'grid'\|'ring', count?, spacing?) -> {ok, spawned:[{actor_path,tag}]}` |
| `playerstart_set_tag` | P2 | python_run-backed TS: src/tools/gameframework/playerstart-set-tag.ts | net-new | Set/replace the PlayerStartTag on an existing PlayerStart so a GameMode's ChoosePlayerStart logic can target it. — `(actor_path, tag) -> {ok, previous_tag}` |
| `playerstart_delete` | P3 | python_run-backed TS: src/tools/gameframework/playerstart-delete.ts | actor_delete exists; framework-scoped convenience | Delete one or more PlayerStart actors (by path or all) — cleanup for re-layout; transaction-wrapped so it's one Ctrl+Z. — `(actor_paths?, all=false) -> {ok, deleted_count}` |
| `gameframework_scaffold` | P2 | python_run-backed TS orchestrating the above + checkpoint: src/tools/gameframework/scaffold.ts | net-new moat (run_tool_script-style single-undo bundle) | One-shot outcome verb: create GameMode + Character/Pawn + PlayerController + HUD (+ optional GameState), wire them all, set as project or world default, and place a PlayerStart — a playable framework from nothing in a single transactional call. — `(base_path, mode:'third_person'\|'top_down'\|'first_person'\|'blank', wire_at:'project'\|'world', place_playerstart=true, plan_approved=false) -> {ok, created:{gamemode,pawn,controller,hud,gamestate?}, wired_at, playerstart?}` |
| `gameframework_apply_preset` | P3 | python_run-backed TS: src/tools/gameframework/apply-preset.ts | DCC steal (Maya prefab generators) | Instantiate a template preset (third-person, top-down, first-person, spectator/UI-only) by cloning sensible base classes and default values; steals the Maya parametric-prefab pattern for gameplay framework. — `(preset:'third_person'\|'top_down'\|'first_person'\|'spectator', target_path, wire=true) -> {ok, preset, created[], wired_at?}` |
| `gameframework_checkpoint` | P2 | python_run-backed TS (snapshot to journal/scratch): src/tools/gameframework/checkpoint.ts | net-new moat (dry_run->commit generalized; transaction safety) | Snapshot the current framework wiring (GameMapsSettings ini values + WorldSettings override + GameMode CDO classes) to a named restore point; required because project-ini edits are outside UE's undo. — `(name?, level_path?) -> {ok, checkpoint_id, captured:{project,world,gamemode}}` |
| `gameframework_restore` | P2 | python_run-backed TS: src/tools/gameframework/restore.ts | net-new moat | Restore framework wiring from a checkpoint — reverts project ini, world override, and GameMode CDO classes together; the only rollback path for the non-undoable ini writes. — `(checkpoint_id) -> {ok, restored:{project,world,gamemode}, diff}` |

**Dependencies:** Hardened python_run handler — every tool is pyTemplate/python-backed, no C++ rebuild; depends on Theme 0/1 SEH+ProcessCommand and the pyTemplate factory (Section 5 #4); unreal.GameMapsSettings CDO (get_default_object + set_editor_property + save_config) for project wiring; GlobalDefaultGameMode/GameInstanceClass ini writes need editor restart to fully take effect; world.get_world_settings() / AWorldSettings.default_game_mode editor property + level_save for per-level overrides; AssetTools/KismetEditorUtilities (create_blueprint, compile_blueprint) + BlueprintGeneratedClass CDO mutation for class-wiring; must mark_package_dirty + compile + save_asset; Existing infra: executeCommand/tool-executor, schema-registry, hayba_asset_search (class discovery), actor_spawn + level_save (PlayerStart), validator_run + plumb_validate (gameframework_validate), Plan-Mode gate before destructive ini writes; Transaction seam (4C C++) for level-actor edits; project-ini edits are NOT in UE undo so handled via checkpoint snapshot files instead

**Risks:** GameMapsSettings.save_config() writes DefaultEngine.ini; GameInstanceClass and (sometimes) GlobalDefaultGameMode changes only fully apply after an editor restart — tools must return a needs_restart warning, not silently imply live effect; Project-ini writes bypass UE's transaction/undo system entirely — Ctrl+Z will NOT revert them; the checkpoint/restore pair is the only rollback path and must be advertised as such; BlueprintGeneratedClass path normalization: BP classes need the _C suffix and CDO mutation; forgetting compile_blueprint + save leaves the wiring un-persisted or stale at next PIE; Setting an abstract or Deprecated class as DefaultPawnClass/PlayerControllerClass yields a silently broken PIE; validate class is concrete + correct base before writing; WorldSettings is per-persistent-level (and World Partition has one) — setting default_game_mode on a streamed sublevel actor is a no-op; must target the persistent level's WorldSettings; Resolution ambiguity: a world override masks the project default; gameframework_verify must reproduce engine precedence exactly or agents will wire the wrong layer and be confused why PIE ignores it; Auto-possess / controller-rotation defaults set on a Pawn CDO can fight an explicitly placed PlayerStart or possession logic, producing a camera that won't move — validate cross-tool consistency

**Live-editor test plan:** On a live editor with a test level open: (1) gameframework_inspect → confirm it reports project GlobalDefaultGameMode, GameInstanceClass, the active level's WorldSettings override, and the resolved effective classes. (2) gamemode_create a BP_TestGameMode, gamemode_set_classes wiring a concrete Character + PlayerController + HUD, then gamemode_get to read back the CDO and confirm persistence after a BP recompile. (3) world_settings_set_gamemode to BP_TestGameMode on the persistent level, level_save. (4) playerstart_spawn at origin with tag 'P1'; verify via playerstart_list and an editor_capture_viewport screenshot showing the PlayerStart gizmo. (5) gameframework_verify → assert the resolution chain returns BP_TestGameMode + its pawn/controller/HUD as effective. (6) gameframework_validate → assert zero issues; then deliberately set an abstract pawn and confirm it flags it. (7) editor_start_pie → confirm the player possesses the wired pawn at the PlayerStart, capture PIE screenshot. (8) gameframework_checkpoint, change the default gamemode, gameframework_restore, re-inspect to confirm the original wiring returns. (9) project_set_default_gamemode → confirm DefaultEngine.ini diff + needs_restart warning surfaced.


<a id="domain-25"></a>
## 25. Source Control integration

*33 tools — P0:10 P1:9 P2:8 P3:6*

UE ships a full provider-agnostic source control layer (ISourceControlModule + ISourceControlProvider, with Perforce/Git/Subversion/PlasticSCM providers), but Hayba exposes ZERO of it as MCP tools — only an internal Slate diff panel touches it (BuildSourceControlBadge/IsSourceControlEnabled). This is a clean net-new moat: no UE-MCP competitor (Aura, GenOrca, remiphilippe, Epic 5.8) exposes editor-side VCS as agent tools, yet it is the single biggest unblocker for unattended, team-safe agent edits — an agent that mutates 40 assets must check them out and submit a coherent changelist, not leave a team's depot in a dirty/locked state. Strategy: ship the read/checkout/add/revert/file-state core as python_run-backed wrappers over unreal.SourceControlHelpers (no rebuild), then do connect-with-credentials, async submit/changelist, resolve, diff, history, and Perforce shelve/lock as one new C++ handler (HaybaMCPSourceControlHandler) driving ISourceControlProvider async operations on the game-thread RunSync seam. Every mutation is inspection-first (paired *_state/_status read), dry-run-able (sc_submit_preview), and routed through the existing Diff panel so the agent's checkouts show up in the same review surface as its asset edits — pressing the crash-resilience + transaction + plan-mode moats directly.

| Tool | Pri | Module | Parity | Description / signature |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `sc_provider_info` | P0 | python_run-backed TS (unreal.SourceControlHelpers.is_available/is_enabled/current_provider) | net-new moat | Report whether source control is enabled/available, which provider is active (Perforce/Git/SVN), and connection/login state — the gating probe every other sc_* tool and the agent should call first. — `() -> {enabled, available, provider, logged_in, workspace, server, user}` |
| `sc_connect` | P0 | C++ HaybaMCPSourceControlHandler (ISourceControlModule.SetProvider + FConnect async op) | net-new moat | Connect/enable a source control provider with credentials (P4 port/user/client or Git remote), persisting to the project's SourceControlSettings — required before any agent VCS op can run unattended. — `(provider, server?, user?, workspace?, password?, args_kv?) -> {ok, logged_in, provider, error_code?}` |
| `sc_disconnect` | P2 | C++ HaybaMCPSourceControlHandler (ISourceControlModule provider teardown) | net-new moat | Disconnect / disable the active provider cleanly (drops the connection without reverting work) — lets an agent tear down credentials at end of a headless session. — `() -> {ok, was_provider}` |
| `sc_status` | P0 | C++ HaybaMCPSourceControlHandler (FUpdateStatus async) with python fast-path via SourceControlHelpers.status | net-new moat | List the source-control status of files under a path/changelist (added/edited/checked-out-by-other/not-current/conflicted), paginated — the inspection-first read that pairs with every mutating tool. — `(path_prefix?, only_modified?, limit=50, offset?) -> {files:[{file, state, other_user?, head_rev, have_rev, in_changelist?}], has_more, next_offset}` |
| `sc_file_state` | P0 | python_run-backed TS (unreal.SourceControlHelpers.get_file_state) | net-new moat | Read back the exact SC state of one or more specific files (checked out by me/other, locked, deleted, current, conflicted) — the verify/read-back primitive used to confirm a checkout/add/revert actually landed. — `(files[] \| package_paths[]) -> {states:[{file, is_checked_out, checked_out_other, is_added, is_deleted, is_current, is_conflicted, is_source_controlled}]}` |
| `sc_refresh` | P1 | C++ HaybaMCPSourceControlHandler (FUpdateStatus with UpdateHistory/Modified flags) | net-new moat | Force-refresh cached SC state for a path or the whole project (FUpdateStatus, update history/modified flags) so subsequent reads aren't stale after external p4/git changes. — `(path_prefix?, update_history=false) -> {refreshed_count}` |
| `sc_checkout` | P0 | python_run-backed TS (unreal.SourceControlHelpers.check_out_file) | net-new moat | Check out (open-for-edit) one or more files/asset packages so the agent can legally mutate them — the gate that prevents read-only-clobber and team conflicts on edited assets. — `(files[] \| package_paths[], silent=true) -> {checked_out[], already_open[], failed:[{file, reason}]}` |
| `sc_checkout_or_add` | P0 | python_run-backed TS (unreal.SourceControlHelpers.check_out_or_add_file) | net-new moat | Check out if tracked, else mark-for-add if new — the single outcome-verb an agent should call before saving any asset it might have just created (Roblox-style 'just make it editable'). — `(files[] \| package_paths[]) -> {checked_out[], added[], failed:[{file, reason}]}` |
| `sc_mark_for_add` | P0 | python_run-backed TS (unreal.SourceControlHelpers.mark_file_for_add) | net-new moat | Mark new untracked files/assets for add so they enter the next changelist — without this an agent's freshly-created assets silently never get committed. — `(files[] \| package_paths[]) -> {added[], failed:[{file, reason}]}` |
| `sc_mark_for_delete` | P1 | python_run-backed TS (unreal.SourceControlHelpers.mark_file_for_delete) | net-new moat | Mark tracked files/assets for delete (open-for-delete) so removal is recorded in VCS rather than an orphaned working-copy delete. — `(files[] \| package_paths[]) -> {deleted[], failed:[{file, reason}]}` |
| `sc_revert` | P0 | python_run-backed TS (unreal.SourceControlHelpers.revert_file / revert_files, FRevert IsRevertUnchanged) | net-new moat — mirrors Aura Sandbox rollback | Revert files (discard checkout/edit, restore depot revision), optionally only-if-unchanged — the agent's undo for VCS-level mistakes and the cleanup after a failed plan. — `(files[] \| package_paths[], only_unchanged=false) -> {reverted[], skipped[], failed[]}` |
| `sc_revert_all` | P1 | C++ HaybaMCPSourceControlHandler (FRevert over enumerated open files) | net-new moat | Revert every file currently open in a changelist (or the default CL) in one call — panic/cleanup verb to return a workspace to clean after an aborted agent run. — `(changelist?='default', dry_run=false) -> {would_revert?[], reverted?[], count}` |
| `sc_changelist_create` | P1 | C++ HaybaMCPSourceControlHandler (FNewChangelist async, ISourceControlChangelist) | net-new moat | Create a named changelist with a description (P4 numbered CL / staged group) so an agent groups one logical task's edits into one reviewable, atomically-submittable unit. — `(description) -> {changelist_id, description}` |
| `sc_changelist_list` | P1 | C++ HaybaMCPSourceControlHandler (GetChangelists) | net-new moat | List pending changelists with id/description/file-count/shelved-flag, paginated — inspection read for choosing where to add files or what to submit. — `(limit=50, offset?) -> {changelists:[{id, description, file_count, shelved}], has_more, next_offset}` |
| `sc_changelist_describe` | P2 | C++ HaybaMCPSourceControlHandler (changelist state + GetFilesInChangelist) | net-new moat | Show one changelist in detail: description plus every file with its action (add/edit/delete) — the pre-submit review/verify read. — `(changelist_id) -> {id, description, files:[{file, action}], shelved_files[]}` |
| `sc_changelist_add_files` | P2 | C++ HaybaMCPSourceControlHandler (FMoveToChangelist async) | net-new moat | Move/assign already-open files into a target changelist so the agent can curate which edits go together before submit. — `(changelist_id, files[]) -> {moved[], failed[]}` |
| `sc_changelist_set_description` | P3 | C++ HaybaMCPSourceControlHandler (FEditChangelist async) | net-new moat | Edit a pending changelist's description (e.g. write a generated commit message summarizing the agent's edits) before submitting. — `(changelist_id, description) -> {ok}` |
| `sc_changelist_delete` | P2 | C++ HaybaMCPSourceControlHandler (FDeleteChangelist async) | net-new moat | Delete an empty pending changelist (after its files were submitted or moved) to keep the workspace tidy. — `(changelist_id) -> {ok, error_code?}` |
| `sc_submit_preview` | P1 | C++ HaybaMCPSourceControlHandler (FUpdateStatus + conflict probe, no FCheckIn) | net-new moat — analog of Flop bp_dry_run | DRY-RUN a submit: list exactly what would be committed (files, actions, CL description) and flag blockers (out-of-date, unresolved, locked-by-other) WITHOUT committing — the safety gate before the irreversible sc_submit. — `(changelist?='default', files?[]) -> {would_submit:[{file, action}], blockers:[{file, reason}], submittable}` |
| `sc_submit` | P0 | C++ HaybaMCPSourceControlHandler (FCheckIn async on RunSync seam) | net-new moat — closes remiphilippe DevOps loop | Submit/commit a changelist (or specific files) with a description — the terminal commit op; honors Plan-Mode and refuses if sc_submit_preview reports unresolved/out-of-date blockers. — `(description, changelist?='default', files?[], require_clean=true) -> {ok, submitted_changelist, file_count, error_code?}` |
| `sc_sync` | P0 | C++ HaybaMCPSourceControlHandler (FSync async) | net-new moat | Sync/get-latest for a path or the whole workspace to a given revision (default head), the agent's 'pull before work' — reports updated/added/deleted and any sync conflicts. — `(path_prefix?, revision?='head', force=false) -> {updated[], added[], deleted[], conflicts[]}` |
| `sc_get_latest` | P2 | python_run-backed TS (SourceControlHelpers.sync_file + AssetRegistry reload) | net-new moat | Convenience outcome-verb: sync only the given files/asset packages to head and reload them in-editor so the agent operates on current content. — `(files[] \| package_paths[]) -> {synced[], reloaded[], failed[]}` |
| `sc_resolve` | P1 | C++ HaybaMCPSourceControlHandler (FResolve async; provider resolve flags) | net-new moat | Resolve conflicted/needs-resolve files using a strategy (accept-mine / accept-theirs / auto-safe) — without this an agent stalls forever on any concurrent-edit conflict. — `(files[], strategy='auto_safe'\|'mine'\|'theirs') -> {resolved[], still_conflicted[], failed[]}` |
| `sc_diff` | P1 | C++ HaybaMCPSourceControlHandler + SHaybaMCPDiffPanel reuse (asset diff via FAssetToolsModule) | net-new moat — extends existing Diff panel | Diff a file against its depot/have revision (or two revisions): for uassets returns a structured object/property diff and feeds the existing Diff panel; for text returns a unified diff — the review primitive before submit. — `(file \| package_path, from_rev?='have', to_rev?='working') -> {kind, changed_props?[], unified_diff?, summary}` |
| `sc_history` | P2 | C++ HaybaMCPSourceControlHandler (FUpdateStatus UpdateHistory + GetHistory) | net-new moat | Show revision history/log for a file (rev, CL, user, date, description) paginated — answers 'who changed this and why' for the agent's reasoning and rollback choices. — `(file \| package_path, limit=20, offset?) -> {revisions:[{rev, changelist, user, date, action, description}], has_more, next_offset}` |
| `sc_blame` | P3 | C++ HaybaMCPSourceControlHandler (provider annotate; P4 'annotate') | net-new moat | Annotate/blame a text file line-by-line with the last-changing revision and author — diagnostic read for code/config conflicts. — `(file, start_line?, end_line?) -> {lines:[{line, rev, user, changelist}]}` |
| `sc_lock` | P3 | C++ HaybaMCPSourceControlHandler (FLock async) | net-new moat | Take an exclusive lock (P4 lock / Git LFS lock) on binary uassets so concurrent agents/humans can't both edit unmergeable files — critical for multi-agent safety on a shared depot. — `(files[] \| package_paths[]) -> {locked[], failed:[{file, reason}]}` |
| `sc_unlock` | P3 | C++ HaybaMCPSourceControlHandler (FUnlock async) | net-new moat | Release locks taken via sc_lock so others can edit — cleanup pair for sc_lock. — `(files[] \| package_paths[]) -> {unlocked[], failed[]}` |
| `sc_shelve` | P3 | C++ HaybaMCPSourceControlHandler (FShelve async) | net-new moat | Shelve a changelist's edits (P4 shelve / Git stash) so an agent can park WIP, hand off for review, or unblock a sync without losing or submitting work. — `(changelist_id, files?[]) -> {shelved[], shelved_changelist}` |
| `sc_unshelve` | P3 | C++ HaybaMCPSourceControlHandler (FUnshelve async) | net-new moat | Restore shelved edits back into the workspace/changelist — pair for sc_shelve, lets an agent resume parked WIP. — `(changelist_id, files?[]) -> {unshelved[], conflicts[]}` |
| `sc_checkout_selected` | P1 | python_run-backed TS (EditorActorSubsystem selection -> packages -> SourceControlHelpers.check_out_or_add_file) | net-new moat — Roblox mass_* analog | Batch outcome-verb: check-out-or-add every asset referenced by the current editor selection (and optionally their dependencies) in one call — the practical entry point before a multi-actor agent edit. — `(include_dependencies=false) -> {checked_out[], added[], failed[], from_selection_count}` |
| `sc_reconcile` | P2 | C++ HaybaMCPSourceControlHandler (status diff + open-for-edit/add/delete) | net-new moat | Reconcile offline work: detect files edited/added/deleted on disk while the agent ran without checkouts, and open them for the correct action — recovers a workspace into a submittable state. — `(path_prefix?, dry_run=true) -> {to_edit[], to_add[], to_delete[], opened?:int}` |
| `sc_settings` | P2 | python_run-backed TS (read SourceControlSettings ini + provider current settings) | net-new moat | Read/inspect the project's source control settings and per-provider config (P4 port/user/workspace, Git remote/branch, auto-checkout-on-modify) — config introspection without round-tripping the UI. — `(set_kv?) -> {provider, settings:{...}, applied?}` |
