| `datatable_row_rename` | P2 | python_run-backed TS (export json -> remap key -> fill replace) | net-new moat | Rename a row key while preserving its values — refactor balance keys without re-authoring. — `(asset_path, old_name, new_name) -> {renamed, new_name}` |
| `datatable_find_rows` | P2 | python_run-backed TS (get_data_table_column_as_string + filter) | net-new moat (search_by_property parity, Roblox peer) | Query rows by column predicate (eq/contains/gt/lt) with pagination — find-by-value without exporting the whole table to context. — `(asset_path, column, op, value, limit?=50, offset?=0) -> {matches:[{row_name,fields}], has_more, next_offset}` |
| `datatable_clear` | P3 | python_run-backed TS (fill_from_json with empty object, replace) | net-new | Empty all rows of a DataTable while keeping the RowStruct — fast reset before a fresh import. — `(asset_path, dry_run?=false) -> {cleared, prior_row_count}` |
| `struct_inspect` | P0 | python_run-backed TS (reflect UScriptStruct via TFieldIterator equivalent / unreal struct property walk) | net-new moat (fixes postmortem 'no reflection-introspection tool' gap) | Introspect any UScriptStruct's fields (name, cpp type, container) — the prerequisite read that lets the agent author correct DataTable/CSV columns and row JSON. — `(struct, include_metadata?=false) -> {struct_path, fields:[{name,type,container,optional}]}` |
| `curve_float_create` | P0 | python_run-backed TS (AssetTools.create_asset + CurveFloatFactory) | GenOrca | Create a CurveFloat asset (RichCurve) — the standard 1D curve for AI, gameplay scaling, and material params. — `(path, name, save?=true) -> {asset_path}` |
| `curve_float_set_keys` | P0 | python_run-backed TS (RichCurve reset + add_key + set_key_interp_mode/tangents) | GenOrca (coarser); net-new tangent+infinity control | Replace a CurveFloat's keys (time, value, interp, optional tangents) in one transaction — authoring the whole curve atomically. — `(asset_path, keys:[{time,value,interp?,arrive_tangent?,leave_tangent?}], pre_infinity?, post_infinity?, dry_run?=false) -> {key_count}` |
| `curve_float_add_key` | P1 | python_run-backed TS (RichCurve.add_key / update key handle) | GenOrca | Add or update a single key on a CurveFloat at a time — incremental curve tweaks. — `(asset_path, time, value, interp?='auto') -> {time, value, key_count}` |
| `curve_float_get` | P0 | python_run-backed TS (RichCurve.get_keys reflection) | net-new moat (paired read tool) | Read back a CurveFloat's keys, interp modes and infinity settings — verify half of curve authoring. — `(asset_path) -> {keys:[{time,value,interp,arrive_tangent,leave_tangent}], pre_infinity, post_infinity}` |
| `curve_float_evaluate` | P1 | python_run-backed TS (CurveFloat.get_float_value) | net-new moat | Sample a CurveFloat at one or many times — numeric verification the curve behaves as designed (validate-after-mutate). — `(asset_path, times[]) -> {samples:[{time,value}]}` |
| `curve_table_create` | P1 | python_run-backed TS (AssetTools.create_asset + CurveTableFactory) | GenOrca | Create a CurveTable asset (rows of curves) — multi-series tabular curves (e.g. per-level stat progressions). — `(path, name, mode?='rich_curves', save?=true) -> {asset_path}` |
| `curve_table_import` | P1 | python_run-backed TS (DataTableFunctionLibrary CurveTable fill / import factory) | GenOrca | Fill a CurveTable from CSV/JSON (row = curve, columns = sample times) — bulk progression-table authoring. — `(asset_path, csv?, json?, csv_file?, json_file?, dry_run?=false) -> {curves_imported, warnings[]}` |
| `curve_table_evaluate_row` | P2 | python_run-backed TS (DataTableFunctionLibrary.evaluate_curve_table_row) | net-new moat | Evaluate a named CurveTable row at given times — read-back/verify of imported progression curves. — `(asset_path, row_name, times[]) -> {row_name, samples:[{time,value}]}` |
| `curve_table_list_rows` | P2 | python_run-backed TS (CurveTable row name reflection) | net-new | List a CurveTable's row names with pagination — enumerate available progression curves. — `(asset_path, limit?=50, offset?=0) -> {rows[], has_more, next_offset, total}` |
| `gameplaytag_add` | P0 | python_run-backed TS (GameplayTagsEditorModule.add_new_gameplay_tag_to_ini if exposed, else ini write + GameplayTagsManager reload) — flag engine-Python ceiling | GenOrca; net-new safe-reload | Register a new GameplayTag (and parents) into DefaultGameplayTags.ini and reload the manager — taxonomy authoring for GAS/abilities. — `(tag, comment?='', source?='DefaultGameplayTags.ini') -> {tag, added, parents_created[]}` |
| `gameplaytag_remove` | P1 | python_run-backed TS (ini edit + reload; AssetRegistry reference scan) | net-new moat (reference-aware delete) | Remove a GameplayTag from its ini source and reload — destructive, Plan-Mode gated, reports references. — `(tag, dry_run?=false) -> {removed, referencing_assets[]}` |
| `gameplaytag_rename` | P2 | python_run-backed TS (write GameplayTagRedirects to ini + reload) | net-new moat | Rename/redirect a GameplayTag via a tag redirect entry — refactor taxonomy without breaking existing references. — `(old_tag, new_tag) -> {renamed, redirect_added}` |
| `gameplaytag_list` | P0 | python_run-backed TS (GameplayTagsManager.request_all_gameplay_tags / get_all_tags) | GenOrca | List registered GameplayTags, optionally filtered by parent/prefix, paginated — inspection-first before adding or referencing tags. — `(prefix?, limit?=50, offset?=0) -> {tags[], has_more, next_offset, total}` |
| `gameplaytag_query` | P1 | python_run-backed TS (GameplayTagsManager.request_gameplay_tag + get_gameplay_tag_children) | net-new moat | Check a tag's registration and enumerate its direct children/descendants — verify taxonomy structure. — `(tag) -> {exists, is_explicit, children[], parent}` |
| `gameplaytag_table_create` | P1 | python_run-backed TS (datatable_create with GameplayTagTableRow + GameplayTagsManager source registration / ini GameplayTagTableList) | net-new moat | Create a GameplayTagTable DataTable (RowStruct=GameplayTagTableRow) and register it as a tag source — table-driven tag taxonomies shippable in content. — `(path, name, register?=true) -> {asset_path, registered}` |
| `gameplaytag_table_add_rows` | P2 | python_run-backed TS (datatable_rows_set_batch over GameplayTagTableRow) | net-new moat | Add tag rows (tag + dev comment) to a GameplayTag table in one transaction — bulk taxonomy authoring in content vs ini. — `(asset_path, tags:[{tag,comment?}], dry_run?=false) -> {written, skipped[]}` |
| `data_asset_create` | P0 | C++ HaybaMCPToolkit DataAssetHandler (existing data_create) wrapped as first-class TS descriptor | GenOrca; existing C++ stub surfaced honestly | Create a UDataAsset or PrimaryDataAsset (native or Blueprint-derived class) — config/data-driven design assets. — `(path, name, class_name, save?=true) -> {asset_path, class}` |
| `data_asset_get` | P0 | C++ HaybaMCPToolkit DataAssetHandler (existing data_get) wrapped as TS descriptor | existing C++ surfaced | Reflect all properties of a DataAsset to JSON — inspection-first read before set. — `(asset_path) -> {asset_path, class, properties:{...}}` |
| `data_asset_set` | P0 | C++ HaybaMCPToolkit DataAssetHandler (existing data_set) wrapped as TS descriptor | existing C++ surfaced | Set one property on a DataAsset from JSON (handles structs/arrays/maps/object refs) with PostEditChange + save. — `(asset_path, property_name, value) -> {asset_path, property, ok}` |
| `data_asset_set_batch` | P1 | python_run-backed TS looping over the C++ data_set seam inside one ScopedEditorTransaction (or new C++ data_set_many) | net-new moat (mass_set_property parity, Roblox peer) | Set many DataAsset properties in one undo step — atomic config edits (named one-Ctrl+Z differentiator). — `(asset_path, properties:{name:value}, dry_run?=false) -> {set[], failed[]}` |
| `data_asset_list` | P1 | python_run-backed TS (AssetRegistry.get_assets_by_class / get_assets_by_path) | net-new moat | Find all DataAssets of a given class/base under a path via AssetRegistry, paginated — enumerate config assets without loading them. — `(class_name?, path?='/Game', recursive?=true, limit?=50, offset?=0) -> {assets[], has_more, next_offset, total}` |
| `data_asset_duplicate` | P2 | python_run-backed TS (EditorAssetLibrary.duplicate_asset) | GenOrca (generic duplicate) | Duplicate a DataAsset/DataTable/Curve to a new path+name — fast variant authoring. — `(source_path, dest_path, dest_name) -> {asset_path}` |
| `primary_asset_query` | P2 | python_run-backed TS (AssetManager.get_primary_asset_id_list / get_primary_asset_path) | net-new moat | Query the Asset Manager for PrimaryAssets by PrimaryAssetType — verify data-driven game registration (loot tables, items). — `(primary_asset_type, limit?=50, offset?=0) -> {assets:[{id,path}], has_more, next_offset}` |
| `structured_data_delete` | P1 | python_run-backed TS (AssetRegistry referencers scan + EditorAssetLibrary.delete_asset) | net-new moat (reference-aware delete) | Safely delete a DataTable/Curve/CurveTable/DataAsset with a reference check — destructive, Plan-Mode gated, dry-run capable. — `(asset_path, dry_run?=true, force?=false) -> {deleted, referencers[]}` |

**Dependencies:** Existing C++ HaybaMCPToolkit DataAssetHandler (data_create/get/set) — surface as first-class descriptors and extend with data_set_many for the batch verb; Hardened python_run handler + runUePythonJson/_emit/_err harness (mcp-tools/hayba-mcp/src/tools/ue-python.ts) — the no-rebuild vehicle for ~32 of 37 tools; UE Python modules: DataTableFunctionLibrary (CSV/JSON fill+export, row names, curve-table eval), AssetTools+factories (DataTableFactory/CurveFloatFactory/CurveTableFactory), AssetRegistry, EditorAssetLibrary, GameplayTagsManager, AssetManager, RichCurve key API; Add-a-tool refactor (Theme 1) ToolDescriptor + pyTemplate factory — these all land as descriptors auto-collected into the data/structured-data pack; Transaction seam (4C ScopedEditorTransaction / C++ FScopedTransaction) for one-undo batch verbs and dry_run->commit generalization; Plan-Mode gate (bPlanApproved) for destructive verbs (remove/clear/delete); PLUMB/validator wrap for dry_run schema validation of CSV/JSON against RowStruct/UScriptStruct

**Risks:** GameplayTag ini authoring is the weakest Python path: IGameplayTagsEditorModule.AddNewGameplayTagToINI may not be reflected to Python in 5.7 — fallback is direct DefaultGameplayTags.ini edit + GameplayTagsManager reload, which risks config races and requires editor reload to fully propagate. Flag as engine-limit; may need a small C++ command in HaybaMCPToolkit.; Single-row DataTable CRUD has no direct Python add/remove-row API in all versions; implementing via export-to-JSON -> splice -> fill-replace is correct but O(table) per edit and loses any non-exported transient state — batch verbs mitigate, but very large tables (10k+ rows) may be slow.; fill_data_table_from_* replaces vs merges semantics differ by version; the replace?/merge behavior must be verified per engine version (pinned 5.7) or rows can be silently dropped — hence dry_run + read-back are P0.; RichCurve key handles in Python are fiddly (tangent/interp enums, auto-tangent recompute on PostEditChange); curve_float_set_keys must reset and re-add deterministically or tangents drift.; struct_inspect on Blueprint UStructs and instanced/EditInline DataAsset properties may hit FJsonObjectConverter '<unsupported:type>' fallbacks (already present in C++ data_get) — nested struct columns may not round-trip losslessly via CSV (JSON only).; Destructive verbs (remove/delete/clear) without the reference-aware scan can orphan GAS abilities or break cooked content — reference checks are mandatory, not optional.; DataTable/Curve fill on the game thread for big imports could approach the command timeout; align with job-envelope ceilings or chunk large imports.

**Live-editor test plan:** Run against a live 5.7 editor with the toolkit plugin loaded. 1) struct_inspect a known native struct (e.g. a project FTableRowBase subclass) and confirm field list matches the C++ definition. 2) datatable_create with that row_struct, datatable_inspect to confirm columns, datatable_import_csv with a 3-row CSV (dry_run first -> expect zero mutation + warnings, then commit), datatable_export json and diff against input, datatable_list_rows + datatable_row_get to verify a specific row, datatable_row_set to update one field then row_get to confirm, datatable_rows_set_batch 5 rows then Ctrl+Z once and confirm all 5 revert (one-undo proof), datatable_row_remove + datatable_find_rows on a column predicate. 3) curve_float_create, curve_float_set_keys with 3 keys + tangents, curve_float_get to read back, curve_float_evaluate at intermediate times and assert interpolation matches expected; open the asset in Curve Editor and eyeball. 4) curve_table_create + curve_table_import + curve_table_evaluate_row. 5) gameplaytag_add a nested tag (A.B.C), gameplaytag_list with prefix='A', gameplaytag_query A.B to confirm child C, then gameplaytag_remove with dry_run reporting referencers; verify DefaultGameplayTags.ini updated and manager reloaded (Project Settings > GameplayTags shows it). 6) data_asset_create a PrimaryDataAsset Blueprint class, data_asset_get, data_asset_set a struct property, data_asset_set_batch then single undo, data_asset_list by class, primary_asset_query by type. 7) structured_data_delete with dry_run=true on a referenced asset -> expect blocked + referencers listed; force delete an unreferenced one. Throughout, confirm no editor freeze (commands return under timeout) and pair each write with its read-back verify tool. Capture before/after via editor_capture_viewport only where the asset is visibly placed; otherwise rely on JSON read-backs.


<a id="domain-31"></a>
## 31. Water system

*36 tools — P0:11 P1:13 P2:10 P3:2*

The UE Water plugin is fully worldbuilding-relevant (oceans, lakes, rivers carving into landscape, animated Gerstner waves) and is completely uncovered by Hayba and every surveyed competitor (Epic 5.8, Aura, Flop, GenOrca 253, ChiR24, remiphilippe) — none ship a single Water tool, so this is almost entirely net-new moat. Nearly all of it is reachable through UE Editor-Scripting Python (unreal.WaterBodyOcean/Lake/River, WaterBodyComponent.set_water_waves, GerstnerWaterWaveGeneratorSimple, AWaterZone, spline point metadata), so the domain ships as python_run-backed TS descriptors under src/tools/water/ with zero plugin rebuild — only the buffer-viz capture and the FScopedTransaction wrap reach into HaybaMCPToolkit C++. We lean into three moats: inspection-first paired read tools, PLUMB quantified validation (water-below-terrain, zone-covers-bodies, sane wavelength/steepness), and outcome-verbs (water_river_from_path, water_ocean_preset_apply) over low-level primitives per DCC best practice. Every mutating tool is transaction-wrapped, supports dry_run, and pairs with a *_inspect read-back so agents validate-before/after-mutate.

| Tool | Pri | Module | Parity | Description / signature |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `water_check_plugin` | P0 | python_run-backed TS: src/tools/water/check-plugin.ts | net-new moat | Probe whether the Water plugin is enabled and report version/subsystem readiness; gates the whole pack so other tools fail with an actionable enable hint, not an ImportError. Why: every WaterBody class is missing without it. — `() -> {ok, enabled, version, water_subsystem_ready, hint?}` |
| `water_body_list` | P0 | python_run-backed TS: src/tools/water/body-list.ts | Flop inspect pattern (net-new for Water) | Paginated introspection of all water bodies in the level with class, zone binding, bounds, and material refs. Why: inspection-first read tool agents need before any mutate. — `(class_filter?, limit=30, offset=0) -> {bodies:[{path,class,label,zone,bounds,has_waves}], has_more, next_offset}` |
| `water_body_inspect` | P0 | python_run-backed TS: src/tools/water/body-inspect.ts | net-new moat | Deep read-back of one water body: component validity, depth/width, wave asset, MID params, physics/affects-landscape flags, owning zone. Why: paired read for validate-before/after-mutate and to catch placeholder-stub spawns. — `(body_path) -> {ok, class, component_valid, waves, materials, affects_landscape, zone, properties}` |
| `water_body_ocean_create` | P0 | python_run-backed TS: src/tools/water/ocean-create.ts | actor-spawn parity (chongdashu/GenOrca); net-new for Water | Spawn an AWaterBodyOcean sized to the landscape/zone with sensible defaults and auto-bind to a WaterZone. Why: the single most common worldbuilding water op; outcome verb over raw actor_spawn. — `(location?, extent?, zone_path?, label?, dry_run=false) -> {ok, body_path, zone, planned?}` |

Why: lakes are a core terrain-water feature, uncovered. — `(location, radius_or_points, water_z?, zone_path?, label?, dry_run=false) -> {ok, body_path, planned?}` |

Why: zone-level analogue of brush_rebuild for the render path. — `(zone_path) -> {ok, updated}` |

Why: visual validate-after-mutate, extends the vision moat (4B). — `(body_path\|zone_path, angles?=4, buffer?:Literal['lit','depth','normal','wireframe'], pie_frame=false) -> {images:[png], camera_meta}` |

Why: one-call art direction; outcome-over-primitive, great agent ergonomics. — `(target_path, preset:Literal, intensity?=1.0, dry_run=false) -> {ok, applied:{waves,material,underwater}, planned?}` |
| `water_zone_mesh_stats` | P3 | python_run-backed TS: src/tools/water/zone-mesh-stats.ts | Unity paginated-hierarchy spirit / net-new | Introspect water render cost: quad-tree/tessellation tile counts, render-target memory, LOD distribution for a zone. Why: unglamorous perf read so agents can right-size resolution before shipping a world. — `(zone_path) -> {ok, tile_count, lod_buckets, render_target_bytes, est_tris}` |

**Dependencies:** Water plugin enabled in the project (preflight via water_check_plugin); A Landscape actor present for lakes/rivers to carve terrain via WaterLandscapeBrush/WaterBrushManager; Rivers require a valid spline path; WaterBodyRiver carries per-point width/depth/velocity metadata; A WaterZone must exist and bound the bodies for rendering (5.7 uses bounded non-tiled zones); Cross-cutting FScopedTransaction seam (Theme 4C / HaybaGameThread::RunSync) for one-Ctrl+Z bundling; PLUMB validator (validator_run / plumb_validate) for water_validate; Vision-capture domain (render_camera / editor_capture_viewport, orbit + buffer passes from 4B) for water_preview_capture; Actor + selection/batch infra (EditorActorSubsystem) for water_bodies_select / water_bodies_batch_set; runUePythonJson plumbing in src/tools/ue-python.ts (HAYBA_JSON contract) for all python-backed tools; Code-Mode / deferred routing + a 'water' pack so the ~30 tools land behind on-demand discovery, not the eager surface

**Risks:** Water plugin not enabled → every unreal.WaterBody* class is missing; water_check_plugin must gate the whole pack and return an actionable enable hint instead of a Python ImportError; UE Python wave coverage is partial: GerstnerWaterWaveGeneratorSimple params are exposed but the spectrum (Phillips/JONSWAP) generator and per-wave arrays may be read-only or EditAnywhere-only — flag spectrum tools as engine-limited, fall back to template WaterWavesAsset instantiation; Editing river spline points + per-point metadata (WaterSplineMetadata) programmatically is fiddly; point count and metadata arrays must stay in lockstep or the body fails to build geometry; Terrain carving requires the WaterBrushManager to re-render the landscape brush; changes can appear stale until a rebuild/dirty — water_brush_rebuild + mark_package_dirty needed, and a full rebuild can be slow (watch the build-freeze class of bug — keep it off the 300s game-thread path or job-envelope it); WaterZone in 5.7 is bounded/non-tiled and owns the water-info render target + MID; resizing or moving bodies outside the zone silently drops rendering — water_validate must assert zone coverage; Water Material/MID edits can hit the same NormalCodeChunk recompile crash class as the material domain; prefer set-on-instance (MID/material instance params) over editing the master water material graph; Spawning AWaterBody via Python may yield a placeholder-like partial actor if the WaterSubsystem/zone isn't initialized (cf. the Landscape spawn placeholder-stub memory) — verify component validity in the read-back, not just spawn success; Many properties are only meaningful at runtime/PIE (wave animation, fluid sim ripples); static editor capture won't show motion — water_preview_capture should note this and optionally drive a short PIE for wave verification

**Live-editor test plan:** On a live editor with the Water plugin enabled and a Landscape present: (1) water_check_plugin → expect ok + plugin version; on a project without it, expect a clean 'enable Water plugin' error not a stack trace. (2) water_body_ocean_create at the landscape center, then water_body_inspect → assert AWaterBodyOcean spawned, has a valid WaterBodyComponent and is bound to a WaterZone. (3) water_zone_inspect → confirm the ocean is inside zone bounds; if no zone, water_zone_create then re-inspect. (4) water_waves_set_gerstner (wavelength/steepness/num_waves/seed), water_waves_inspect → read back the generator params match. (5) editor_capture_viewport / water_preview_capture orbit around the ocean → visually confirm water surface renders (and note motion only shows in PIE; optionally editor_start_pie for a wave screenshot). (6) water_body_river_create from a 4-point path over the landscape, water_river_spline_get → assert point count + per-point width/depth/velocity; water_brush_rebuild → screenshot the carved riverbed. (7) water_body_lake_create, then water_validate → expect green (water below terrain at shoreline, zone covers all bodies, wavelength/steepness in sane range); deliberately move the lake above terrain and re-run → expect a red unsat-core finding. (8) water_bodies_select(class=WaterBodyLake) + water_bodies_batch_set(target_wave_mask_depth) across selection, then Ctrl+Z once → assert all reverted in a single undo (transaction bundling). (9) every create/set tool with dry_run:true first → assert it reports the planned change and mutates nothing, then commit.
